converges a.s. on the set

\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\left(|X_{i}|^{p}|\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right)<\infty\right\}
.

Theorem 8.3

(Theorem 2.18 from
Hall and Heyde (2014)
)
.

Let
\left\{S_{n}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i},\mathcal{F}_{n},n\geq 1\right\}
be a martingale and
\left\{U_{n},n\geq 1\right\}
a non-decreasing sequence of positive random variables such that
U_{n}
is
\mathcal{F}_{n-1}
-measurable for each
n
. If
1\leq p\leq 2
, then

\lim_{n\to\infty}U_{n}^{-1}S_{n}=0\ a.s.

on the set
\left\{\lim_{n\to\infty}U_{n}=\infty,\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}U_{i}^{-p}\mathbb{E}\left(|X_{i}|^{p}|\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right)<\infty\right\}
.

We also state the Toeplitz Lemma from
Hall and Heyde (2014)
.

Lemma 8.1
.

For
i\geq 1
, if
x_{i}
are real numbers,
a_{i}
are positive numbers, and
b_{n}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}
diverges, then
x_{n}\to x
ensures that

b_{n}^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}x_{i}\to x.

Lastly, we state a strong law of large numbers specific to the branching process model developed in Section
2
. Assume the historical information and epoch setup of Section
2
for the following Theorem.

Theorem 8.4
.

For function
z:\mathscr{D}\to\mathbb{R}
, assume for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}
,
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}
, and
B<\infty
that

\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{z^{2}({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}<B.

(10)

Additionally, assume that for
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
\{{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}
are conditionally independent given
\mathcal{F}_{j-1}
.
For
\delta>0
, define the event
E_{\mathscr{I}}=\left\{\mathscr{I}>\delta\right\}
, where
\mathscr{I}
is defined in Assumption
5
. Then,

\lim_{J\to\infty}\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]\to 0

on the event
E_{\mathscr{I}}
.

Proof.

We first consider the sum

\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}.

This is clearly a martingale sequence with respect to fields
\left\{\mathcal{F}_{j}\right\}_{j=1}^{J}
and martingale differences

\left[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]_{j=1}^{J}.

Consequently, we can invoke Theorem
8.3
, implying that

\lim_{J\to\infty}\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]=0

on the set

\displaystyle\Bigg\{\lim_{J\to\infty}\overline{\kappa}_{J}\to\infty,

\displaystyle\lim_{J\to\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}^{2}_{j}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left(\left[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right)<\infty\Bigg\}.

We examine

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{j}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left(\left[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right)\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{j}^{2}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left(\left[W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right)=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{j}^{2}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W^{2}_{i}z^{2}({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}-

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]\Big)\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{j}^{2}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left(W^{2}_{i}z^{2}({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right)=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{j}^{2}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\int W_{i}^{2}z^{2}({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})f\left({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i}|\mbox{\boldmath$A$}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right)\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\pi}}({\mathbf{A}}_{i}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1})d\nu\overset{(b)}{=}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{j}^{2}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\int\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\widetilde{\pi}}({\mathbf{A}}_{i}|{\mbox{\boldmath$H$}}_{i})}{\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\pi}}({\mathbf{A}}_{i}|{\mbox{\boldmath$H$}}_{i})}z^{2}({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})f\left({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i}|\mbox{\boldmath$A$}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right)\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\pi}}({\mathbf{A}}_{i}|{\mbox{\boldmath$H$}}_{i})d\nu=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{j}^{2}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left(z^{2}({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right)\overset{(c)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{\kappa_{j}}{\overline{\kappa}_{j}^{2}}B\overset{(d)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{\kappa_{j}}B.

Equality
(a)
follows from the fact that for
i>g
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left(\left[W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]\left[W_{g}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{g})-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{g}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{g})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\Bigg(\left[\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{g}\right\}-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]\times

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\left[W_{g}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{g})-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{g}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{g})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\Bigg(\left[\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]\times

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\left[W_{g}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{g})-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{g}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{g})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=0.

Equality
(i)
follows because

\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{g}\right\}=\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}z({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}.

Equality
(b)
follows from the fact that
\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\pi}}({\mathbf{A}}_{i}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1})=\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\pi}}({\mathbf{A}}_{i}|{\mbox{\boldmath$H$}}_{i})
and the definition of
W_{i}
.
Inequality
(c)
follows from Equation
10
.
Additionally, we know

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{\kappa_{j}}B

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{m^{j-1}}\frac{m^{j-1}}{\kappa_{j}}B

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{m^{j-1}}\right)\left(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{m^{j-1}}\right)^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{m^{j-1}}\frac{m^{j-1}}{\kappa_{j}}B

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{a.s.}}{\to}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{m^{j-1}}\right)\mathscr{I}^{-1}B

by Lemma
8.1
and Assumption
5
.
Because we assume event
E_{\mathscr{I}}
, we know that
\mathscr{I}^{-1}<\frac{1}{\delta}
. Consequently,

\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{\kappa_{j}}B\overset{\mathrm{a.s.}}{\to}\frac{1}{1-1/m}\mathscr{I}^{-1}B<\frac{B}{\delta(1-1/m)}<\infty.

\blacksquare

8.2
Consistency

First, we show asymptotic consistency of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}
. We follow the proof of
Zhang et al. (2021)
, making slight changes to account for a more general context. We define
e_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\triangleq W_{i}l_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})
. By the definition of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}
,

\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}e_{i}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})=\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}e_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right)\geq\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}e_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right).

(11)

Note that
\|\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|>\epsilon>0
implies that

\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}:\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|>\epsilon}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}e_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right)=\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}e_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right).

(12)

Define
\mathbb{P}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}
as the probability distribution associated with the complete data-generating distribution, where
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}
is the generative parameter associated with the branching process and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}
is the policy followed during data collection. Equations
11
and
12
imply the following inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left(\|\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}:\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|>\epsilon}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}e_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right)\geq\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}e_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip-71.13188pt=\mathbb{P}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left[\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}:\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|>\epsilon}\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right)-\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\geq 0\right]

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt=\mathbb{P}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\Bigg[\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}:\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|>\epsilon}\Bigg\{\Bigg(\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\Bigg[W_{i}l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right)-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right)|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}+

\displaystyle\hskip-99.58464pt\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right)|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\Bigg]\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip-99.58464pt-\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\left[W_{i}l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}+\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]\geq 0\Bigg\}\Bigg].

By the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\leq\mathbb{P}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\Bigg\{&\underbrace{\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}:\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|>\epsilon}\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\left[W_{i}l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right)-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right)|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]\right)}_{\mathrm{(a)}}\\
&+\underbrace{\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}:\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|>\epsilon}\left\{\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left[W_{i}\left\{l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right)-l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\right\}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]\right)\right\}}_{\mathrm{(b)}}\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt-\underbrace{\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\left[W_{i}l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]}_{\mathrm{(c)}}\geq 0\Bigg\}.\end{split}

(13)

We first analyze quantity (c), which is a martingale by construction. By Assumption
8
(the moments condition), we know that there exists
B_{l^{2}}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that

\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{l_{i}^{2}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\leq B_{l^{2}}
. Consequently,
by Theorem
8.4
,
\lim_{J\to\infty}|\mathrm{(c)}|\to 0
almost surely. We use a uniform martingale strong law of large numbers, Lemma
8.2
, to prove that
\lim_{J\to\infty}|\mathrm{(a)}|\to 0
almost surely.

Therefore, it is sufficient for consistency to show that there exists
\delta^{\prime}>0
such that

\lim_{J\to\infty}\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}:\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|>\epsilon}\left\{\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left[W_{i}\left\{l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right)-l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\right\}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]\right)\right\}\leq-\delta^{\prime}\ \mathrm{a.s.}

By the law of iterated expectations,

\displaystyle\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}:\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|>\epsilon}\left\{\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left[W_{i}\left\{l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right)-l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\right\}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]\right)\right\}=

\displaystyle\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}:\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|>\epsilon}\Bigg\{\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\Big(\int_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\mathbb{P}\left({\mathbf{A}}_{i}={\mathbf{a}}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right)\times

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188ptW_{i}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}}\left\{l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right)-l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\mid{\mathbf{A}}_{i}={\mathbf{a}},\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}d{\mathbf{a}}\Big)\Bigg\}.\ \ (*)

First, by the restriction of information explained in Section
2
, we know that

\mathbb{P}\left({\mathbf{A}}_{i}={\mathbf{a}}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right)=\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left({\mathbf{a}}\mid H_{i}\right)
. For
\rho_{\min},\rho_{\max}\in\mathbb{R}
, we know that
\rho_{\min}\leq W_{i}\leq\rho_{\max}
by Assumption
4
.
Because
\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}}\left\{l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right)-l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)|{\mathbf{A}}_{i}={\mathbf{a}}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\leq 0
with probability
1
, we find that

\displaystyle(*)\ \leq\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}:\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|>\epsilon}\Bigg\{\frac{1}{\rho_{\max}\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\Bigg(\int_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}({\mathbf{a}}\mid{\mbox{\boldmath$H$}}_{i})\times

\displaystyle\hskip 204.85974ptW_{i}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}}\left\{l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right)-l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)|{\mathbf{A}}_{i}={\mathbf{a}},\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}d{\mathbf{a}}\Bigg)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}:\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|>\epsilon}\Bigg\{\frac{1}{\rho_{\max}\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\Bigg(\int_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\mathbb{P}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}({\mathbf{a}}\mid{\mbox{\boldmath$H$}}_{i})\times

\displaystyle\hskip 204.85974pt\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}}\left\{l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right)-l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)|{\mathbf{A}}_{i}={\mathbf{a}},\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}d{\mathbf{a}}\Bigg)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}:\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|>\epsilon}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{\max}\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right)-l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right].

From Assumption
10
, it follows that

\frac{1}{\rho_{\max}}\lim_{J\to\infty}\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}:\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|>\epsilon}\left[\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right)-l_{i}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]\leq-\delta\frac{1}{\rho_{\max}}\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

Thus, Equation
13
holds with
\delta^{\prime}\triangleq\delta\frac{1}{\rho_{\max}}
.

8.3
Convergence Rate

First, we will prove that

\Sigma_{J}^{1/2}\left(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)=O_{p}\left(1\right).

(14)

Define the weighted log-likelihood at
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}
as

\mathcal{M}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\triangleq\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}l_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}).

We begin with a Taylor series expansion of
\dot{\mathcal{M}_{J}}
between
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}
and
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}
,

0=\dot{\mathcal{M}_{J}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})+\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J})(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}),

where
\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J}
is between
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}
and
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}
. Multiplying and dividing by
\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}
, we find that

\displaystyle-\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\dot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\left\{\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J})\right\}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{J}^{1/2}\left(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right).

If we show that

\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\dot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})=O_{p}(1),

(15)

and

\sigma_{\min}\left(\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J})\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\right)^{-1}=O_{p}\left(1\right),

(16)

then Equation
14
follows.
Consequently, we will divide the proof of Equation
14
into proofs of Equations
15
and
16
. This proof only relies on Assumptions
1
-
8
and
11
.

8.3.1
Proof of Equation
15

We first prove that

\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\dot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})=O_{p}(1).

Note that

\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\dot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})=\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\right\}.

Define
{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
as a standard basis vector with
1
in the
g^{th}
position and
0
elsewhere. We know that for any
g\in\{1,2,\ldots,k\}
,

\left\{{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}^{\top}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\right\}_{j=1}^{J}

is a martingale difference sequence with respect to fields
\left\{\mathcal{F}_{j}\right\}_{j=1}^{J}
. For any
j\in\mathbb{N}
and
\boldsymbol{\pi}\in\Pi
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\boldsymbol{\pi}}\left\{{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}^{\top}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}

\displaystyle={\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}^{\top}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\boldsymbol{\pi}}\left\{W_{i}\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\boldsymbol{\pi}}\left[{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}^{\top}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}}\left\{\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1},{\mathbf{A}}_{i}\right\}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]

\displaystyle=0.

We now apply Theorem
8.2
(Theorem 2.15 from
Hall and Heyde (2014)
) to this martingale difference sequence. By this theorem,

\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left[\left\{{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}^{\top}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\right\}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]\overset{p}{\to}{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}^{\top}U{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}.

is sufficient for

\sum_{j=1}^{J}{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}^{\top}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})

to converge almost surely. We find that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left[\left\{{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}^{\top}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\right\}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left[\left\{{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}^{\top}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{\left(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\right)\left(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})^{\top}\right)\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}^{\top}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\left\{W_{i}^{2}\dot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\dot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})^{\top}\right\}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]\right\}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}^{\top}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\widetilde{\pi}}({\mathbf{A}}_{i})}{\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\pi}}({\mathbf{A}}_{i})}\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})^{\top}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right\}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}

\displaystyle={\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}^{\top}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\Bigg\{\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\int\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\widetilde{\pi}}({\mathbf{A}}_{i}|{\mbox{\boldmath$H$}}_{i})}{\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\pi}}({\mathbf{A}}_{i}|{\mbox{\boldmath$H$}}_{i})}\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})^{\top}\times

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188ptf\left({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i}|\mbox{\boldmath$A$}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{j-1};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\pi}}({\mathbf{A}}_{i}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1})d\nu\Bigg\}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{=}{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}^{\top}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\Bigg\{\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\int\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\widetilde{\pi}}({\mathbf{A}}_{i}|{\mbox{\boldmath$H$}}_{i})}{\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\pi}}({\mathbf{A}}_{i}|{\mbox{\boldmath$H$}}_{i})}\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})^{\top}\times

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188ptf\left({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i}|\mbox{\boldmath$A$}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{j-1};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\pi}}({\mathbf{A}}_{i}|{\mbox{\boldmath$H$}}_{i})d\nu\Bigg\}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}

\displaystyle={\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}^{\top}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\int\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})^{\top}f\left({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i}|\mbox{\boldmath$A$}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{j-1};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\mathbb{P}_{\widetilde{\pi}}({\mathbf{A}}_{i}|{\mbox{\boldmath$H$}}_{i})d\nu\right\}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}

\displaystyle={\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}^{\top}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})^{\top}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{=}{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}^{\top}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\eta_{J}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}.

Equality
(a)
follows from the law of iterated expectations applied to the cross terms of the sum (as explained in the proof of Theorem
8.4
); equality
(b)
follows from the definition of
W_{i}
; equality
(c)
follows from the definition of historical information,
{\mbox{\boldmath$H$}}_{i}
; and equality
(d)
follows from the definition of
\eta_{J}
.
By Assumption
11
,

{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}^{\top}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\eta_{J}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}\overset{p}{\to}{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}^{\top}U{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}.

By Theorem
8.2
, this implies that

{\mbox{\boldmath$c$}}_{g}^{\top}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})

converges a.s. to a random variable, which we label
V_{g}
for
g\in\left\{1,2,\ldots,k\right\}
. Labeling
{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}=(V_{1},V_{2},\ldots,V_{k})
, we observe that

\lim_{J\to\infty}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\overset{p}{\to}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}.

This verifies equation
15
.

8.3.2
Proof of Equation
16

First, note that the differentiability and moment conditions of the log-likelihood (Assumptions
7
and
8
) imply that

\eta_{J}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\dot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{-\ddot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}.

Define

\alpha_{J}\triangleq\sum_{j=1}^{J}-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\ddot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}.

By Lemmas
11.2
and
11.3
and the fact that
\rho_{\min}\leq W_{i}\leq\rho_{\max}
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}\left(\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\left\{-\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J})\right\}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sigma_{\min}\left(\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\left\{-\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J})+\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\right\}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\right)+

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\sigma_{\min}\left(\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\left\{-\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-\alpha_{J}\right\}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\right)+

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\sigma_{\min}\left(\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\alpha_{J}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sigma_{\min}\left\{\left(\Sigma_{J}/\overline{\kappa}_{J}\right)^{-1/2}\right\}\sigma_{\min}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J})-\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\right)\times

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\sigma_{\min}\left\{\left(\Sigma_{J}/\overline{\kappa}_{J}\right)^{-1/2}\right\}+

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\sigma_{\min}\left\{\left(\Sigma_{J}/\overline{\kappa}_{J}\right)^{-1/2}\right\}\sigma_{\min}\left\{\frac{-\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-\alpha_{J}}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\right\}\times

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\sigma_{\min}\left\{\left(\Sigma_{J}/\overline{\kappa}_{J}\right)^{-1/2}\right\}+

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\sigma_{\min}\left(\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\alpha_{J}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\geq}\sigma_{\min}\left\{\left(\Sigma_{J}/\overline{\kappa}_{J}\right)^{-1/2}\right\}\sigma_{\min}\left\{\frac{\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J})-\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\right\}\times

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\sigma_{\min}\left\{\left(\Sigma_{J}/\overline{\kappa}_{J}\right)^{-1/2}\right\}+

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\sigma_{\min}\left\{\left(\Sigma_{J}/\overline{\kappa}_{J}\right)^{-1/2}\right\}\sigma_{\min}\left\{\frac{-\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-\alpha_{J}}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\right\}\times

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\sigma_{\min}\left\{\left(\Sigma_{J}/\overline{\kappa}_{J}\right)^{-1/2}\right\}+

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\frac{1}{\rho_{\max}}\sigma_{\min}\left(\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\eta_{J}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\right).

By Assumption
11
,
\sigma_{\min}\left(\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\eta_{J}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\right)^{-1}=O_{p}(1)
as
J\to\infty
. Inequality (a) will be verified by Property (4) below. In sum, it is sufficient to establish four properties:

1.

\sigma_{\min}\left\{\left(\Sigma_{J}/\overline{\kappa}_{J}\right)^{-1/2}\right\}\leq\left\|\left(\Sigma_{J}/\overline{\kappa}_{J}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}=O_{p}(1)

2.

\left\|\left\{\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J})\right\}/\overline{\kappa}_{J}\right\|_{2}=o_{p}(1)
.

3.

\left\|\left\{-\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-\alpha_{J}\right\}/\overline{\kappa}_{J}\right\|_{2}=o_{p}(1)

4.

\alpha_{J}\succeq\frac{1}{\rho_{\max}}\eta_{J}

Properties (1)-(3) ensure that

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}\left\{\left(\Sigma_{J}/\overline{\kappa}_{J}\right)^{-1/2}\right\}\sigma_{\min}\left\{\frac{\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J})-\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\right\}\sigma_{\min}\left\{\left(\Sigma_{J}/\overline{\kappa}_{J}\right)^{-1/2}\right\}=o_{p}(1),\ \mathrm{and}

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}\left\{\left(\Sigma_{J}/\overline{\kappa}_{J}\right)^{-1/2}\right\}\sigma_{\min}\left\{\frac{-\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-\alpha_{J}}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\right\}\sigma_{\min}\left\{\left(\Sigma_{J}/\overline{\kappa}_{J}\right)^{-1/2}\right\}=o_{p}(1).

This allows us to conclude that

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}\left(\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\left\{-\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J})\right\}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{\max}}\sigma_{\min}\left\{\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\eta_{J}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\right\}+o_{p}(1)+o_{p}(1)\right]^{-1}

\displaystyle=O_{p}(1).

We begin with Property (1).
By Assumption
12
, there exists
\epsilon>0
such that

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}(\eta_{J}/\overline{\kappa}_{J})\geq\epsilon+o_{p}(1)\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\overline{\kappa}_{J}\|\eta_{J}^{-1}\|_{2}\leq 1/\epsilon+o_{p}(1).

By Assumption
11
,
\left\|\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\eta_{J}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}=O_{p}(1)
.
Consequently, by the sub-multiplicativity of the spectral norm,

\displaystyle\overline{\kappa}_{J}\left\|\Sigma_{J}^{-1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\overline{\kappa}_{J}\left\|\Sigma_{J}^{-1}\eta_{J}\eta^{-1}_{J}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\overline{\kappa}_{J}\left\|\Sigma_{J}^{-1}\eta_{J}\right\|_{2}\left\|\eta^{-1}_{J}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq O_{p}(1)\left\{\frac{1}{\epsilon}+o_{p}(1)\right\}

as
J\to\infty
.
Note that the last equality follows because the eigenvalues of
\Sigma_{J}^{-1}\eta_{J}
are the same as
\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\eta_{J}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}
.

We now prove Property (2).

We analyze

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J})}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\left\{\ddot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-\ddot{l}_{i}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J})\right\}}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\|\ddot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-\ddot{l}_{i}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J})\|_{2}}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}},

where the last inequality follows from the triangle inequality. We wish to show that for any
\epsilon>0
,

\lim_{J\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\|\ddot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-\ddot{l}_{i}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J})\|_{2}}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}>\epsilon\right)=0.

(17)

Recall that Assumption
13
implies there exists an
\epsilon_{\ddot{l}}>0
and a function
f:\mathscr{D}\to\mathbb{R}
such that for all

0<\epsilon^{*}\leq\epsilon_{\ddot{l}}
, there exists
\delta_{\epsilon^{*}}
such that

\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N},i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1},\ {\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}:\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\leq\delta_{\epsilon^{*}}}\|\ddot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-\ddot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\|_{2}\leq f({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\epsilon^{*}\ \ \mathrm{a.s.},

and
\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{f^{2}({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}
is bounded almost surely.
Because
\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J}
is consistent for
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}
, we know that for any
\delta_{\epsilon^{*}}
,
\lim_{J\to\infty}\|\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|\leq\delta_{\epsilon^{*}}
a.s. Consequently,

\displaystyle\lim_{J\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\|\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-\ddot{l}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J})\|_{2}}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{J\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\epsilon^{*}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}f({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}>\epsilon\right).

We now analyze the quantity

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}f({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}f({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}f({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}f({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}.

We know that

\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}f({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}f({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}=o_{p}(1)

(18)

by Theorem
8.4
because Assumption
13
states that
\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{f^{2}({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}
is bounded almost surely.
Therefore,

\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}f({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})=o_{p}(1)+\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}f({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}.

By Assumption
4
, we know that
\rho_{\min}\leq W_{i}\leq\rho_{\max}
. Because
f({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})
is a positive function, we can conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}f({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})

\displaystyle\leq o_{p}(1)+\frac{1}{\rho_{\min}\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}^{2}f({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq o_{p}(1)+B_{f}/\rho_{\min}.

Defining
\epsilon^{*}\triangleq\epsilon\rho_{\min}/(2B_{f})
, we find that

\displaystyle\lim_{J\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\|\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-\ddot{l}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J})\|_{2}}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{J\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\epsilon\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}f({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{J\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(\epsilon\rho_{\min}/(2B_{f})

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\left\{o_{p}(1)+B_{f}/\rho_{\min}\right\}>\epsilon\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{J\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(o_{p}(1)>\epsilon/2\right)\to 0.

Consequently, Property (2) is satisfied.

We now prove Property (3).

We verify the following condition,

\lim_{J\to\infty}\left\|\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}-W_{i}\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}-W_{i}\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\right\|_{2}=0\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

By Assumption
8
(the moments assumption) and Assumption
4
, for any
p,q\in[k]
, there exists
B_{\ddot{l}^{2}}
such that for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
and
i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{[\ddot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})]_{p,q}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq B_{\ddot{l}^{2}}\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

Consequently, we know that

\left\|\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}-W_{i}\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-\alpha_{J}}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\right\|=o_{p}(1)

by Theorem
8.4
(component-wise).

Lastly, we prove Property (4).
First, we find that

\displaystyle\alpha_{J}

\displaystyle\triangleq-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left[W_{i}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}}\left\{-\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\mid{\mathbf{A}}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right].

We know
\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}}\left\{-\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\mid{\mathbf{A}}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\succeq 0
(because this is equivalent to the variance of the score function), and therefore we can express

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left[W_{i}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}}\left\{-\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\mid{\mathbf{A}}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]

\displaystyle\succeq\frac{1}{\rho_{\max}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left[W^{2}_{i}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}}\left\{-\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\mid{\mathbf{A}}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho_{\max}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}}\left\{-\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\mid{\mathbf{A}}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\rho_{\max}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho_{\max}}\eta_{J}.

This satisfies Property (4).
We have verified Equation
16
.

Equation
14
,

\Sigma_{J}^{1/2}\left(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)=O_{p}\left(1\right),

follows from Equations
15
and
16
.
Equation
14
is used to conclude that

\left(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)=O_{p}\left(1/\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\right)

by showing

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\left(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\overline{\kappa}_{J}^{1/2}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{J}^{1/2}\left(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\overline{\kappa}_{J}^{1/2}\Sigma_{J}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}\left\|\Sigma_{J}^{1/2}\left(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=O_{p}(1).

The last equality follows from Property (1) and Equation
14
.

Lemma 8.2
.

Let
e_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\triangleq W_{i}l_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})
. Under Assumptions
1
-
13
,

\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}}\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\left[e_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{e_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]\right)=o_{p}(1).

Lemma
8.2
is a martingale uniform law of large numbers, and its proof mirrors Lemma 2 of
Zhang et al. (2021)
.

Proof.

Like
Zhang et al. (2021)
, we begin by establishing a finite bracketing number for the log-likelihood function based on Assumption
9
and the fact that
\mathcal{B}
is bounded.

Finite Bracketing Number.

Let
\delta>0
. We construct a set
B_{\delta}
that is made up of pairs of functions
(b,u)
and satisfies the following criteria.

1.

We denote
l_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})
as
l({\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})
to emphasize its dependence on data
{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i}\in\mathscr{D}
.
We show that for any
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}
, we can find functions
(b,u)\in B_{\delta}
such that
b({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\leq l({\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\leq u({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})
and

\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N},i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{u({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-b({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\leq\delta\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

2.

There are a finite number of pairs in
B_{\delta}
,
|B_{\delta}|<\infty
.

3.

For any
(b,u)\in B_{\delta}
, there exists
m_{g}<\infty
that does not depend on
\delta
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N},i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{b({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\leq m_{g}\ \ \mathrm{a.s},\ \mathrm{and}

\displaystyle\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N},i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{u({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\leq m_{g}\ \ \mathrm{a.s}.

We now construct
B_{\delta}
. Create a grid over
\mathcal{B}
with a meshwidth of
\lambda>0
, and let the points in this grid be the set
G_{\lambda}\subseteq\mathcal{B}
. By construction, this implies that for any
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}
, we can find
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\prime}\in G_{\lambda}
such that
\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\prime}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|\leq\lambda
.

By Assumption
9
, we know that for any
{\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}
,
j\in\mathbb{N}
, and
i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}
, there exists function
g
and constant
m_{g}<\infty
such that
|l({\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-l({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\prime},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})|\leq g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\prime}\|_{2}
and

\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\leq m_{g}\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

(19)

We specify
B_{\delta}=\left\{(l({\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-\lambda g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i}),l({\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})+\lambda g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})):{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in G_{\lambda}\right\}
and show that this set satisfies properties (1)-(3) for a certain value of
\lambda
. Note that because
\mathcal{B}
is bounded, property (2) is satisfied because the number of points in
G_{\lambda}
is finite.
To show that (1) holds for
B_{\delta}
, recall that for any
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}
, we can find
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\prime}\in G_{\lambda}
such that
\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\prime}\|\leq\lambda
. Define
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\lambda}^{\prime}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\triangleq\min_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\prime}\in G_{\lambda}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\prime}\right\|
.
By Assumption
9
, this implies that

\left|l({\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-l\left\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\lambda}^{\prime}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}),{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i}\right\}\right|\leq g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\lambda}^{\prime}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\|_{2}\leq g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\lambda.

Therefore,

l\left\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\lambda}^{\prime}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}),{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i}\right\}-\lambda g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\leq l({\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\leq l\left\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\lambda}^{\prime}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}),{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i}\right\}+\lambda g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i}),

implying that there exists
(b,u)\in B_{\delta}
such that
b({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\leq l({\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\leq u({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})
for all
{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i}\in\mathcal{D}
.
Additionally, note that for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
and
i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left[\left|l\left\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\lambda}^{\prime}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}),{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i}\right\}+\lambda g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-\left[l\left\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\lambda}^{\prime}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}),{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i}\right\}-\lambda g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\right]\right|\Big|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]

\displaystyle=2\lambda\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left(|g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})||\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right)\leq 2\lambda\sqrt{m_{g}}\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

The inequality above holds by Jensen’s inequality,

\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{|g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})||\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}}\ \mathrm{a.s.,}

and Equation
19
. We conclude that (1) holds for our choice of
B_{\delta}
by letting the meshwidth
\lambda\triangleq\delta/(2\sqrt{m_{g}})
.

Lastly, we show that (3) holds. Note that for any
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N},i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left[\left\{l({\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})+\lambda g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\right\}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N},i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{l({\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}+2\lambda^{2}\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N},i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

by the triangle inequality and the fact that for any
p,q\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle(p+q)^{2}

\displaystyle=p^{2}+2pq+q^{2}

\displaystyle=2p^{2}+2q^{2}-p^{2}+2pq-q^{2}

\displaystyle=2p^{2}+2q^{2}-(p-q)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2p^{2}+2q^{2}.

By the same logic,

\displaystyle\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N},i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left[\left\{l({\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-\lambda g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\right\}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N},i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{l({\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}

\displaystyle+2\lambda^{2}\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N},i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

Because
l({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}})=l({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}})-l({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})+l({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})
, we upper bound

\displaystyle 2\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N},i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{l({\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}+2\lambda^{2}\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N},i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 4\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N},i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{l({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}+4\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N},i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left[\left\{l({\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-l({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\right\}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 165.02606pt+2\lambda^{2}\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N},i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

Note that
\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N},i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\leq m_{g}
a.s., and
\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{l({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}
is bounded by Assumption
8
.

By Assumption
9
, for any
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}
,

\displaystyle|l({\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-l({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})|\leq g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\left\{l({\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-l({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\right\}^{2}\leq g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left[\left\{l({\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-l({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\right\}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}
is bounded because
\mathcal{B}
is bounded, and
\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N},i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{g({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\leq m_{g}
a.s. We conclude that property (3) holds.
We now use
B_{\delta}
in the main argument for the proof of uniform convergence.

Main Argument:

We now show that for any
\epsilon>0
,

\mathbb{P}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}}\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\left[e_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{e_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]\right)>\epsilon\right\}=o(1).

(20)

Let
\delta>0
(we will choose
\delta
later). Let
B_{\delta}
be the set of pairs of functions as constructed earlier. Note that by property (1) of
B_{\delta}
, we get the following upper bound,

\displaystyle\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}}\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\left[W_{i}l_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}l_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{(b,u)\in B_{\delta}}\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\left[W_{i}u({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}b({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]\right)\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}\ \ (*)

By subtracting and adding
\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}u({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}
, and using the triangle inequality, we find that

\displaystyle(*)\ \leq

\displaystyle\max_{(b,u)\in B_{\delta}}\left[\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left[W_{i}\left\{u({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-b({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\right\}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]\right]+

\displaystyle\max_{(b,u)\in B_{\delta}}\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\left[W_{i}u({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}u({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]\right)\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

By Assumption
4
and the fact that
u({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-b({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left[W_{i}\left\{u({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-b({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\rho_{\min}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left[W_{i}^{2}\left\{u({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-b({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho_{\min}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{u({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-b({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\rho_{\min}}\delta.

The last inequality holds by property (1) of
B_{\delta}
. Because
\max_{i\in[n]}a_{i}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}|a_{i}|
,

\displaystyle\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}}\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\left[W_{i}l_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}l_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\rho_{\min}}\delta+\sum_{(b,u)\in B_{\delta}}\left|\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\left[W_{i}u({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}u({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]\right|.

By property (3) of
B_{\delta}
, we know that

\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}^{2}u({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}=\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{u({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\leq m_{g}.

Therefore, by Theorem
8.4
, for any
(b,u)\in B_{\delta}
,

\left|\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\left[W_{i}u({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}u({\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i})|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}\right]\right|=o_{p}(1).

Because
|B_{\delta}|<\infty
by Property (2), the convergence holds for all
(b,u)\in B_{\delta}
simultaneously, so

(*)\leq\frac{1}{\rho_{\min}}\delta+o_{p}(1).

Equation
20
is satisfied by choosing
\delta=\rho_{\min}\epsilon/2
.

\blacksquare

9
Proof of Theorem
2.2

We establish asymptotic regret bounds for RL-RDS by proving Theorem
2.2
.
The following consequence of Markov’s inequality will be useful.

Lemma 9.1
.

Let
\{X_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
be a sequence of random variables where
X_{n}\in\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
||\cdot||
is an arbitrary norm on
\mathbb{R}^{p}
. If
\mathbb{E}||X_{n}||=O(1)
for all
n\geq 1
, then
\left\{X_{n}\right\}_{n\geq 1}=O_{p}(1)
.

Proof.

If
\mathbb{E}||X_{n}||=O(1)
for all
n\geq 1
,
\exists M>0
such that
\forall n\geq 1
,

\mathbb{E}||X_{n}||\leq M.

We need to show that given
\epsilon
,
\exists V_{\epsilon}
such that
P(||X_{n}||\geq V_{\epsilon})\leq\epsilon
for all
n\geq 1
. We know that

\mathbb{P}(||X_{n}||\geq V_{\epsilon})\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}||X_{n}||}{V_{\epsilon}}\leq\frac{M}{V_{\epsilon}}

by the Markov Inequality. Choosing
V_{\epsilon}=M/\epsilon
gives the desired result.

\blacksquare

In Assumption
6
, we define
S^{n}
as the budget left when individual
n
is recruited, where
S^{n}
satisfies
0<S^{n}-\sum_{v=1}^{n}C^{v}<C^{*}
for
C^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
and each
n\in\mathbb{N}
. We define an upper bound on the
potential number of additional recruits under allocation
strategy
\boldsymbol{\pi}
at any state
{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}}
for
J\in\mathbb{N}
as

n^{*}(\boldsymbol{\pi},{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{k})\triangleq\inf\left\{n\,:\,\sum_{v=k}^{n}C^{v*}(\boldsymbol{\pi},{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{k})>C^{*}\right\}.

We assume that there exists
S^{*}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for any
{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}}\in\mathcal{H}^{n_{J}}
,

\sup_{\boldsymbol{\pi}\in\Pi}n^{*}(\boldsymbol{\pi},{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}})\leq S^{*}
a.s.

Proof of Theorem
2.2
.

\displaystyle V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})

\displaystyle=V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})+V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})

\displaystyle\leq V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{J};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})+V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})

\displaystyle=V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{J};{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}})+V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{J};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})-V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{J};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})+

\displaystyle\hskip 17.07182ptV^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})

\displaystyle\leq V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{J};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})+2\sup_{\boldsymbol{\pi}\in\Pi}\left|V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})\right|,

where the first inequality follows from the definition of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{J}
. The upper bound above does not depend on
\boldsymbol{\pi}
, and so it holds for
\boldsymbol{\pi}^{\mathrm{opt}}
,

V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi}^{\mathrm{opt}};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{J};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\leq 2\sup_{\boldsymbol{\pi}\in\Pi}|V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})|.

We know that
V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi}^{\mathrm{opt}};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{J};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\geq 0
because
\boldsymbol{\pi}^{\mathrm{opt}}\in\arg\max_{\boldsymbol{\pi}\in\Pi}V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})
, so

|V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi}^{\mathrm{opt}};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{J};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})|\leq 2\sup_{\boldsymbol{\pi}\in\Pi}|V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})|.

We now show that
\sup_{\boldsymbol{\pi}\in\Pi}|V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})|=O_{p}(1/\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}})
, which proves the desired result. We expand
V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})
as follows. Recall that
\Delta^{v}
is an indicator that the budget has not been exceeded when individual
v
enters the study. Note that
\exists S^{*}
such that we can express

\displaystyle\begin{split}&V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})=\\
&\int\left(\sum_{k=1}^{S^{*}}\Delta^{k}y^{k}\right)\left\{\prod_{m=1}^{S^{*}}f({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m+1}|{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m},{\mathbf{a}}^{m};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\pi({\mathbf{a}}^{m}|{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m})\right\}d\lambda({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{S^{*}})-\\
&\int\left(\sum_{k=1}^{S^{*}}\Delta^{k}y^{k}\right)\left\{\prod_{m=1}^{S^{*}}f({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m+1}|{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m},{\mathbf{a}}^{m};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})\pi({\mathbf{a}}^{m}|{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m})\right\}d\lambda({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{S^{*}}),\end{split}

(21)

where
{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{1}={\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}}
.
Refactoring this expression and applying the definition of the log-likelihood results in

\displaystyle V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})=

\displaystyle\int\left(\sum_{k=1}^{S^{*}}\Delta^{k}y^{k}\right)\left\{1-\frac{\prod_{m=1}^{S^{*}}f({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m+1}|{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m},{\mathbf{a}}^{m};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})\pi({\mathbf{a}}^{m}|{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m})}{\prod_{m=1}^{S^{*}}f({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m+1}|{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m},{\mathbf{a}}^{m};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\pi({\mathbf{a}}^{m}|{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m})}\right\}\times

\displaystyle\prod_{m=1}^{S^{*}}f({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m+1}|{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m},{\mathbf{a}}^{m};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\pi({\mathbf{a}}^{m}|{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m})d\lambda({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{S^{*}})=

\displaystyle\int\left(\sum_{k=1}^{S^{*}}\Delta^{k}y^{k}\right)\left[1-\exp\left\{\ell_{S^{*}}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})-\ell_{S^{*}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\right\}\right]\times

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\left\{\prod_{m=1}^{S^{*}}f({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m+1}|{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m},{\mathbf{a}}^{m};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\pi({\mathbf{a}}^{m}|{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m})\right\}d\lambda({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{S^{*}}).

By Assumption
14
, we know that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\left|\ell_{S^{*}}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})-\ell_{S^{*}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\sum_{v=1}^{S^{*}}|q^{v}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})-q^{v}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})|

\displaystyle\leq\left[\sum_{v=1}^{S^{*}}|e\left\{(R^{v},T^{v},{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v},Y^{v},{\mathbf{A}}^{v})\right\}|\right]\left\|\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\left(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\right\|_{2}.

We know
\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})=O_{p}(1)
by Theorem
2.1
. Labeling
e^{v}\triangleq e\left\{(R^{v},T^{v},{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v},Y^{v},{\mathbf{A}}^{v})\right\}
and noting that
e^{v}
is strictly positive, we also know that for any
\boldsymbol{\pi}\in\Pi
,

\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\boldsymbol{\pi}}\left(|e^{v}|\right)=O(1)

(22)

by Jensen’s Inequality.
By Lemma
9.1
, this implies that
\sum_{v=1}^{S^{*}}\left|e^{v}\right|=O_{p}(1)
. We conclude that

\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\left|\ell_{{S^{*}}}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})-\ell_{{S^{*}}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\right|=O_{p}(1)\Rightarrow\left|\ell_{{S^{*}}}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})-\ell_{{S^{*}}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\right|=O_{p}(1/\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}).

By Taylor expansion,

1-\exp\left\{\ell_{S^{*}}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})-\ell_{S^{*}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\right\}=\ell_{S^{*}}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})-\ell_{S^{*}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})+o_{p}\left(1/\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\right).

(23)

Because
Y^{v}\in[0,1]
for all
v\in\mathbb{N}
,

\left|\sum_{k=1}^{S^{*}}Y^{k}\Delta^{k}\right|=\sum_{k=1}^{S^{*}}|Y^{k}\Delta^{k}|\leq S^{*},

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}|(\ref{eq:Int})|\leq&S^{*}\times\left|\ell_{S^{*}}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})-\ell_{S^{*}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})+o_{p}\left(1/\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\right)\right|\\
&\times\left\{\prod_{m=1}^{S^{*}}f({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m+1}|{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m},{\mathbf{a}}^{m};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\pi({\mathbf{a}}^{m}|{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m})\right\}d\lambda({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{S^{*}}).\end{split}

(24)

Leveraging equations (
22
), (
23
), and (
24
) as well as the Cauchy-Schwartz Inequality, we know that as
J\to\infty

\displaystyle|(\ref{eq:Int})|\leq

\displaystyle\ {S^{*}}\times\frac{1}{\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}}\left\|\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\left\{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right\}\right\|_{2}\left\{\sum_{v=1}^{S^{*}}|e^{v}|\right\}

\displaystyle\times\left\{\prod_{m=1}^{{S^{*}}}f({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m+1}|{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m},{\mathbf{a}}^{m};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\pi({\mathbf{a}}^{m}|{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m})\right\}d\lambda({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{{S^{*}}})+

\displaystyle o_{p}\left(1/\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\right)\times\frac{1}{\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}}\left\|\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\left\{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right\}\right\|_{2}\left\{\sum_{v=1}^{S^{*}}|e^{v}|\right\}

\displaystyle\times\left\{\prod_{m=1}^{{S^{*}}}f({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m+1}|{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m},{\mathbf{a}}^{m};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\pi({\mathbf{a}}^{m}|{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{m})\right\}d\lambda({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{{S^{*}}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{S^{*}}\times\frac{1}{\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}}\left\|\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\left\{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right\}\right\|_{2}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{\sum_{v=1}^{S^{*}}|e^{v}|\right\}+

\displaystyle o_{p}\left(1/\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\right)\times\frac{1}{\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}}\left\|\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\left\{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right\}\right\|_{2}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{\sum_{v=1}^{S^{*}}|e^{v}|\right\}.

This follows because
\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})=O_{p}(1)
, and
\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left(\sum_{v=1}^{S^{*}}|e^{v}|\right)=O(1)
.

Therefore,
|V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-V^{n_{J}}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n_{J}},\boldsymbol{\pi};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})|=O_{p}(1/\sqrt{\overline{\kappa}_{J}})
as
J\to\infty
. This proves the result.

\blacksquare

10
Epoch Asymptotics

As mentioned in Section
2.1
in the main text, we observe the branching process data in order of arrival time,
\mathcal{D}^{\kappa}\triangleq\left\{\left(R^{v},T^{v},{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v},Y^{v},{\mathbf{A}}^{v},C^{v}\right)\right\}_{v=1}^{\kappa}
. Define
J_{\kappa}
as the last complete epoch induced by coupon expiration,
J_{\kappa}\triangleq\max\{j:\forall i\in\mathcal{E}_{j},\ T_{i}+t_{\max}<T^{\kappa}\}
. Lemma
10.1
ensures that
\lim_{\kappa\to\infty}J_{\kappa}\to\infty
a.s. under Assumption
5
, implying that inference based on the last complete epoch will achieve the asymptotic guarantees of Theorem
2.3
.

Lemma 10.1
.

Assume that the working model specified in Equation
6
is the true generative model. Under Assumption
5
,

J_{\kappa}\to\infty\ \mathrm{a.s.}

Proof.

First, we show that

\lim_{\kappa\to\infty}T^{\kappa}=\infty.

Define
V_{S}
as the seed sample set. Under Assumption
5
, the number of coupons in a coupon allocation is upper bounded by
L_{*}
. It is sufficient to show that for any
g\in\mathbb{N}
,
T^{|V_{S}|L_{*}^{g-1}+1}\geq gt_{\min}
.

We employ a proof by induction. First, we show the base case. Suppose
g=1
. We know that
T^{|V_{S}|+1}\geq t_{\min}
by Assumption
5
. We now assume that for any
g\in\mathbb{N}
,

T^{|V_{S}|*L_{*}^{g-1}+1}\geq gt_{\min}.

Note that
t_{\min}>0
.
Consequently, an upper bound on the number of active coupons in the branching process at time
T^{|V_{S}|*L_{*}^{g-1}}
is

|V_{S}|L_{*}^{g-1}*L_{*}=|V_{S}|L_{*}^{g}.

Note that any study participant recruited by a new member of the sample (an individual recruited after the first
|V_{S}|L_{*}^{g-1}
study participants) will have a recruitment time greater than
(g+1)t_{\min}
; i.e., for any
\kappa
such that
R^{\kappa}>|V_{S}|L_{*}^{g-1}
,
T^{\kappa}\geq(g+1)t_{\min}
.
Because
|V_{S}|L_{*}^{g}-|V_{S}|L_{*}^{g-1}
is an upper bound on the number of active coupons at time
T^{|V_{S}|L_{*}^{g-1}}
, we know that

R^{|V_{S}|L_{*}^{g}}\geq|V_{S}|L_{*}^{g-1}
. Consequently,

T^{|V_{S}|L_{*}^{g}+1}>(g+1)t_{\min}.

We conclude that

\lim_{\kappa\to\infty}T^{\kappa}=\infty

by induction. Now we establish that

\lim_{\kappa\to\infty}J_{\kappa}=\infty.

Recall that
\overline{\kappa}_{J}\triangleq|\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{J}|
.
Additionally, recall that
|{\mathbf{A}}^{v}|\leq L_{*}
for all
v\in N
.
Consequently, for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
\left|\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{j}\right|\leq L_{*}^{j}<\infty
.
This implies that
\max_{i\in\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{j}}T_{i}\leq t_{\max}L_{*}^{j}
. Consequently, for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
,

1=P\left(\lim_{\kappa\to\infty}\left\{t_{\max}L_{*}^{j}+t_{\max}<T^{\kappa}\right\}\right)\leq P\left(\lim_{\kappa\to\infty}\left\{\forall i\in\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{j},\ T_{i}+t_{\max}<T^{\kappa}\right\}\right).

We conclude that
\lim_{\kappa\to\infty}J_{\kappa}=\infty
.

\blacksquare

11
Proof of Theorem
2.3

In this section, we prove Theorem
2.3
for the branching model described in Equation
6
.
Under Conditions (C1)-(C6), we verify Assumptions
4
,
5
, and
7
-
13
. This will allow us to invoke Theorems
2.1
and
2.2
for the branching model described by Equation
6
.

General notation for this section.

For matrix
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
,
\sigma_{\min}(A)
and
\sigma_{\max}(A)
are the minimum and maximum singular values of
A
respectively. Define
\mathrm{vec}(A)
as the vectorization of
A
. For a symmetric matrix
X\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, define
\mathrm{vech}(X)
as the vectorization of the lower-triangular elements of
X
.
Label
I_{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
as the
d
-dimensional identity matrix.

11.1
The Branching Models Derivatives

Before we begin verifying assumptions, it will be useful to calculate the derivatives of the log-likelihood of the branching process specified in Equation
6
. First, we define the data

{\mbox{\boldmath$D$}}_{i}=\left(\underline{{\mbox{\boldmath$Y$}}}_{i},\underline{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}}_{i},\underline{{\mbox{\boldmath$T$}}}_{i},M_{i},{\mathbf{A}}_{i}\right).

For example,
{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i}
is the covariate vector of recruiter
i
, and
\underline{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}}_{i}=\left({\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i,1},{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i,2},\ldots,{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i,M_{i}}\right)
are the recruits of recruiter
i
.
The complete
likelihood for the branching process is

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{L}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\triangleq\\
&\mathcal{L}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{y})\mathcal{L}_{J}(\left\{\boldsymbol{\phi}_{{\mathbf{a}}},G_{a},\Sigma_{a}\right\}_{a\in\mathbb{A}})\mathcal{L}_{J}(\zeta,\lambda)\triangleq\\
&\prod_{j=1}^{J}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\prod_{l=1}^{M_{i}}\left[\frac{1}{1+\exp(-{{\mbox{\boldmath$Z$}}_{i,l}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{y})}\right]^{Y_{i,l}}\left[\frac{1}{1+\exp({{\mbox{\boldmath$Z$}}_{i,l}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{y})}\right]^{1-Y_{i,l}}\times\\
&\prod_{j=1}^{J}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\prod_{l=1}^{M_{i}}(2\pi)^{-p/2}|\sum_{a\in\mathbb{A}}\Sigma_{a}\mathbb{I}\left(A_{i,l}=a\right)|^{-1/2}\times\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{A}}\left\{({\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i,l}-\boldsymbol{\phi}_{a}-G_{a}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i})^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}_{a}({\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i,l}-\boldsymbol{\phi}_{a}-G_{a}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i})\right\}\mathbb{I}(A_{i,l}=a)\right]\\
&\prod_{j=1}^{J}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}M_{i}!\left[\frac{\prod_{l=1}^{M_{i}}\zeta e^{-\zeta U_{i,l}}}{[e^{-\zeta t_{\min}}-e^{-\zeta t_{\max}}]^{M_{i}}}\right]\frac{\lambda^{M_{i}}/M_{i}!}{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}^{v}|}(\lambda^{\ell}/\ell!)}.\end{split}

(25)

Lastly, we rewrite the logarithm of the part of the likelihood that involves
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}=\zeta
and
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{m}=\lambda
as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\ell_{J}(\zeta,\lambda)\triangleq&\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\sum_{l=1}^{M_{i}}\left\{\log(\zeta)-\zeta U_{i,l}-\log\left(e^{-\zeta t_{\min}}-e^{-\zeta t_{\max}}\right)\right\}+\\
&\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\left[M_{i}\log(\lambda)-\log\{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}(\lambda^{\ell}/\ell!)\}\right].\end{split}

(26)

We will need the hessian of the log-likelihood of this branching process for the proofs that follow. Recall that
\mathbb{A}
is the set of possible coupon types. We note that each coupon allocation is a set of identical coupons, implying that the sets
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathbb{A}
have a one-to-one correspondence. Consequently, for
{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}
, there exists
a\in\mathbb{A}
such that

{\mathbb{I}}({\mathbf{A}}_{i}={\mathbf{a}})={\mathbb{I}}(A_{i,l}=a)

for every
l\in\left\{1,2,\ldots,M_{i}\right\}
. Consequently, we can represent the complete branching process likelihood as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{L}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\triangleq\\
&\mathcal{L}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{y})\mathcal{L}_{J}(\left\{\boldsymbol{\phi}_{{\mathbf{a}}},G_{{\mathbf{a}}},\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}})\mathcal{L}_{J}(\zeta,\lambda)\triangleq\\
&\prod_{j=1}^{J}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\prod_{l=1}^{M_{i}}\left[\frac{1}{1+\exp(-{{\mbox{\boldmath$Z$}}_{i,l}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{y})}\right]^{Y_{i,l}}\left[\frac{1}{1+\exp({{\mbox{\boldmath$Z$}}_{i,l}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{y})}\right]^{1-Y_{i,l}}\times\\
&\prod_{j=1}^{J}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\prod_{l=1}^{M_{i}}(2\pi)^{-p/2}|\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathbb{A}}\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}_{i}={\mathbf{a}})|^{-1/2}\times\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathbb{A}}\left\{({\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i,l}-\boldsymbol{\phi}_{{\mathbf{a}}}-G_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i})^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}_{{\mathbf{a}}}({\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i,l}-\boldsymbol{\phi}_{{\mathbf{a}}}-G_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i})\right\}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}_{i}={\mathbf{a}})\right]\\
&\prod_{j=1}^{J}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}M_{i}!\left[\frac{\prod_{l=1}^{M_{i}}\zeta e^{-\zeta U_{i,l}}}{[e^{-\zeta t_{\min}}-e^{-\zeta t_{\max}}]^{M_{i}}}\right]\frac{\lambda^{M_{i}}/M_{i}!}{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}^{v}|}(\lambda^{\ell}/\ell!)}.\end{split}

(27)

We will use this likelihood for the proofs that follow.

The Hessian for the log-likelihood of the covariate model.

Define
{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i}^{*}=\left(1,{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i}\right)
and
G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}=\left(\phi_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{\top},G_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{\top}\right)^{\top}
. The likelihood of the covariate model parameter,

{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}=\left\{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}},\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}
, is

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{J}\left(\left\{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}},\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\right)

\displaystyle\triangleq\prod_{j=1}^{J}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\prod_{l=1}^{M_{i}}(2\pi)^{-p/2}\left|\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\mathbb{I}(c={\mathbf{a}})\right|^{-1/2}\times

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\left\{({\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i,l}-G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{*}_{i})^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}_{{\mathbf{a}}}({\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i,l}-G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{*}_{i})\right\}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}_{i}={\mathbf{a}})\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=1}^{J}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\prod_{l=1}^{M_{i}}(2\pi)^{-p/2}\left|\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}_{i}={\mathbf{a}})\right|^{-1/2}\times

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\exp\Bigg[-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\Big\{{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i,l}}^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i,l}-2{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{*}_{i}}^{\top}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i,l}+

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{*}_{i}}^{\top}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}_{{\mathbf{a}}}G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{*}_{i}\Big\}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}_{i}={\mathbf{a}})\Bigg].

The equality above simply follows from distributing.
For
{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}
