\displaystyle\lim_{J\to\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}M_{i}\left(\frac{1}{\zeta^{2}}+\frac{ae^{-\zeta a}-b^{2}e^{-\zeta b}}{[e^{-\zeta a}-e^{-\zeta b}]^{2}}\right)\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]/t_{J-1}=

\displaystyle\lim_{J\to\infty}\left(\overline{\kappa}_{J}/t_{J-1}\right)m\left(\frac{1}{\zeta^{2}}+\frac{ae^{-\zeta a}-b^{2}e^{-\zeta b}}{[e^{-\zeta a}-e^{-\zeta b}]^{2}}\right)=

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}m\left(\frac{1}{\zeta^{2}}+\frac{ae^{-\zeta a}-b^{2}e^{-\zeta b}}{[e^{-\zeta a}-e^{-\zeta b}]^{2}}\right)\geq

\displaystyle\delta^{*}m\left(\frac{1}{\zeta^{2}}+\frac{ae^{-\zeta a}-b^{2}e^{-\zeta b}}{[e^{-\zeta a}-e^{-\zeta b}]^{2}}\right)\ \mathrm{a.s.}

where the last inequality follows from the fact that there exists
\delta_{*}
such that
\mathscr{I}>\delta_{*}
by assumption. Defining

\delta_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}}\triangleq\delta^{*}m\left(\frac{1}{\zeta^{2}}+\frac{ae^{-\zeta a}-b^{2}e^{-\zeta b}}{[e^{-\zeta a}-e^{-\zeta b}]^{2}}\right)

completes the first part of the lemma.

For any
\tau\in\mathcal{B}
, we find that

\displaystyle\lim_{J\to\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}\left\{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}{\tau}^{2}\frac{\left\{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}e^{{\tau}\ell}/\ell!\right\}\left\{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}\ell^{2}e^{{\tau}\ell}/\ell!\right\}-\left\{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}\ell e^{{\tau}\ell}/\ell!\right\}^{2}}{\left\{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}e^{{\tau}\ell}/\ell!\right\}^{2}}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}/t_{J-1}\geq

\displaystyle\lim_{J\to\infty}\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}{\tau_{J-1}}{\tau}^{2}\frac{\left\{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}e^{{\tau}\ell}/\ell!\right\}\left\{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}\ell^{2}e^{{\tau}\ell}/\ell!\right\}-\left\{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}\ell e^{{\tau}\ell}/\ell!\right\}^{2}}{\left\{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}e^{{\tau}\ell}/\ell!\right\}^{2}}\geq

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}{\tau}^{2}\frac{\left\{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}e^{{\tau}\ell}/\ell!\right\}\left\{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}\ell^{2}e^{{\tau}\ell}/\ell!\right\}-\left\{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}\ell e^{{\tau}\ell}/\ell!\right\}^{2}}{\left\{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}e^{{\tau}\ell}/\ell!\right\}^{2}}\geq

\displaystyle\delta^{*}{\tau}^{2}\frac{\left\{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}e^{{\tau}\ell}/\ell!\right\}\left\{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}\ell^{2}e^{{\tau}\ell}/\ell!\right\}-\left\{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}\ell e^{{\tau}\ell}/\ell!\right\}^{2}}{\left\{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}e^{{\tau}\ell}/\ell!\right\}^{2}}\ \mathrm{a.s.}

where the last inequality follows from the fact that there exists
\delta_{*}
such that
\mathscr{I}>\delta_{*}
by assumption. Defining

\delta_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{m}}\triangleq\delta^{*}{\tau}^{2}\frac{\left\{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}e^{{\tau}\ell}/\ell!\right\}\left\{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}\ell^{2}e^{{\tau}\ell}/\ell!\right\}-\left\{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}\ell e^{{\tau}\ell}/\ell!\right\}^{2}}{\left\{\sum_{\ell=k}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}e^{{\tau}\ell}/\ell!\right\}^{2}}

completes the proof of the lemma.

\blacksquare

11.3.5
Assumption
12

Assumption
12
follows from the fact that
\Sigma_{J}=t_{J-1}
, verification of Assumption
11
and Lemmas
11.8
-
11.10
,

\lim_{J\to\infty}\eta_{J}/t_{J-1}=\mathscr{I}\mathbb{C}

where
\mathbb{C}\succ 0
w.p. 1. Consequently,

\frac{\eta_{J}}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}=\frac{\eta_{J}}{t_{J-1}}\frac{t_{J-1}}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\overset{p}{\to}\mathbb{C}\mathscr{I}\mathscr{I}^{-1}=\mathbb{C}\succ 0

by the continuous mapping theorem. We conclude that
Assumption
12
is satisfied.

11.3.6
Assumption
13

We prove
Assumption
13
.
Set any
\epsilon_{\ddot{l}}>0
. We conceptualize
\ddot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})
as a function
\ddot{\lambda}:\mathcal{B}\times\mathscr{D}\to\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
. From Section
11.2
, we know that
\ddot{\lambda}
is continuous over both
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathscr{D}
. Because
\mathcal{X}
is compact, we know that
\mathscr{D}
is compact. Consequently,
\ddot{\lambda}
is uniformly continuous in
\mathcal{B}\times\mathscr{D}
(since
\mathcal{B}
is compact too). Therefore, for any
0<\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{\ddot{l}}
, we can find
\delta_{\epsilon}
such that for any
{\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\prime}
and
{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}},{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}^{\prime}
such

\sup_{\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\prime}\|_{2}+\|\textbf{d}-\textbf{d}^{\prime}\|_{2}\leq\delta_{\epsilon}}\left\|\ddot{\lambda}({\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}})-\ddot{\lambda}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\prime},{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}^{\prime})\right\|_{2}\leq\epsilon.

Since
\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}},{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}^{\prime}:\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\leq\delta_{\epsilon},\|{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}-{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}^{\prime}\|_{2}=0\}\subseteq\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\prime},{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}},{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}:\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\prime}\|_{2}+\|{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}-{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}^{\prime}\|_{2}\leq\delta_{\epsilon}\}
, we know that

\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}:\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|_{2}\leq\delta_{\epsilon}}\sup_{\textbf{d}\in\mathscr{D}}\left\|\ddot{\lambda}({\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}})-\ddot{\lambda}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}})\right\|_{2}\leq\epsilon.

Assumption
13
is satisfied.

11.4
Consistency Proof

Note that we do not need Assumptions
9
and
10
in the proof of consistency for the branching process described in Equation
6
.
We begin with a finite Taylor series expansion,

0=\dot{\mathcal{M}_{J}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})+\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J})(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}),

where
\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J}
is between
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}
and
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}
.
By the concavity of exponential families
(
Brown, 1986
)
– this can also be observed in the proofs of Lemmas
11.8
-
11.10
– we know that for all
J\in\mathbb{N}
and
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}
,
\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})/t_{J-1}
is invertible. Consequently,

\displaystyle-\dot{\mathcal{M}_{J}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})/t_{J-1}=\left\{\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J})/t_{J-1}\right\}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle-\left\{\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J})/t_{J-1}\right\}^{-1}\dot{\mathcal{M}_{J}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})/t_{J-1}=\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}.

Under Assumptions
1
-
8
and Assumption
11
, we know that

\dot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})/t_{J-1}^{1/2}=O_{p}(1)

by Section
8.3.1
.
Consequently,

\dot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})/t_{J-1}=o_{p}(1).

Therefore, we only need to show

\left\{-\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{J})/t_{J-1}\right\}^{-1}=O_{p}(1)

to prove that

\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}=o_{p}(1).

To do this, we show that for any
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}
,

\sigma_{\min}\left\{-\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})/t_{J-1}\right\}\geq\epsilon^{*}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}/\rho_{\max}+o_{p}(1).

(30)

This implies that

\left\|\left\{-\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})/t_{J-1}\right\}^{-1}\right\|_{2}\leq\rho_{\max}/\epsilon^{*}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}+o_{p}(1).

To show Equation
30
, we first define

\displaystyle\nu_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})=-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\ \Bigg|\ \mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\},

\displaystyle\alpha_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})=-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\ \Bigg|\ \mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}

By Lemmas
11.8
-
11.10
, we know that (on the event
E_{\mathscr{I}}
) for any
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}
and

\epsilon^{*}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}=\min\left\{\delta_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{m}^{*}},\delta_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}^{*}},\delta_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}^{*}},\delta_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{y}^{*}}\right\}
(where
\delta_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{m}^{*}},\delta_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}^{*}},\delta_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}^{*}},\delta_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{y}^{*}}
are defined in Lemmas
11.8
-
11.10
),

\lim_{J\to\infty}\nu_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})/t_{J-1}\geq\epsilon^{*}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}.

(31)

By Lemmas
11.2
and
11.3
,

\displaystyle\lim_{J\to\infty}\sigma_{\min}\left\{\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})/t_{J-1}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\rho_{\max}}\lim_{J\to\infty}\sigma_{\min}\left\{\left\{-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\ddot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\right\}\alpha_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{-1}\nu_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})/t_{J-1}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\geq\frac{1}{\rho_{\max}}\lim_{J\to\infty}\sigma_{\min}\left\{\left\{-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\ddot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\right\}\alpha_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{-1}\right\}\sigma_{\min}\left\{\nu_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})/t_{J-1}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt=\frac{1}{\rho_{\max}}\lim_{J\to\infty}\sigma_{\min}\left\{\left\{-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\ddot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\right\}\alpha_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{-1}-I+I\right\}\sigma_{\min}\left\{\nu_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})/t_{J-1}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\geq\frac{1}{\rho_{\max}}\lim_{J\to\infty}\left(\sigma_{\min}\left\{\left\{-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\ddot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\right\}\alpha_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{-1}-I\right\}+1\right)\sigma_{\min}\left\{\nu_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})/t_{J-1}\right\}.

The first inequality follows from the inequality

\alpha_{J}\succ\frac{1}{\rho_{\max}}\nu_{J}.

(32)

Equation
32
follows from the fact that
{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})
is concave (because each component of the branching process is a member of a full exponential family – this can also be observed in the proofs of Lemmas
11.8
-
11.10
),
-\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\succeq 0
, so for every
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}
,

\displaystyle\alpha_{J}

\displaystyle\triangleq\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W_{i}(-\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}))\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}

\displaystyle\succeq\frac{1}{\rho_{\max}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{W^{2}_{i}(-\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}))\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho_{\max}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}\left\{(-\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}))\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right\}.

Additionally, from Property (3) of Section
8
, we know that

\lim_{J\to\infty}\left\|\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\ddot{l}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})-\alpha_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\right\|_{2}=0\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

(33)

We can extend this to

\displaystyle\left\|\left\{-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\right\}\alpha_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{-1}-I\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left\{\left\{-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\right\}-\alpha_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\right\}\alpha_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{-1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left\{\left\{-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\right\}-\alpha_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\right\}\right\|_{2}\left\|\alpha_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{-1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\rho_{\max}\left\|\frac{\left\{\left\{-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{E}_{j-1}}W_{i}\ddot{l}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\right\}-\alpha_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})\right\}}{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}\right\|_{2}\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}{t_{J-1}}\left\|\nu_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{-1}\right\|_{2}t_{J-1}

\displaystyle=o_{p}(1)\left\{O_{p}(1)+o_{p}(1)\right\}\left\{\frac{1}{\epsilon^{*}_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}}+o_{p}(1)\right\}

\displaystyle=o_{p}(1).

The third to last equality follows from Equation
32
. The second to last equality follows from Equation
31
, Equation
33
, and the fact that

\lim_{J\to\infty}\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{J}}{t_{J-1}}=\mathscr{I}\ \mathrm{a.s.}

Consequently, we know that

\sigma_{\min}\left\{-\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})/t_{J-1}\right\}\geq\epsilon_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{*}/\rho_{\max}+o_{p}(1).

This implies that

t_{J-1}\left\|\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{-1}\right\|_{2}\leq\rho_{\max}/\epsilon_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{*}+o_{p}(1).

Note that
\left\|\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{-1}\right\|_{2}
is a continuous function of
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
by Section
11.1
. Because
\mathcal{B}
is compact, we know that

\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}}t_{J-1}\left\|\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{-1}\right\|_{2}\leq\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}}\rho_{\max}/\epsilon_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{*}+o_{p}(1)<\infty

by the extreme value theorem. Consequently, we know that

t_{J-1}\left\|\ddot{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J})^{-1}\right\|_{2}=O_{p}(1).

This demonstrates that
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{J}
is consistent.

11.5
Proof of Theorem
2.3

We have proved consistency and verified Assumptions
5
-
8
and Assumptions
11
-
14
. This proves Theorems
2.1
and
2.2
. Theorem
2.3
follows from Theorems
2.1
and
2.2
.

12
Generalized RDS Inference

12.1
Hessian of the Covariate Model

The Hessian for the log-likelihood of the covariate model.

Define
{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{*}^{v}\triangleq\left(1,{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}\right)
and
G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}=\left(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{a}^{\top},G_{a}^{\top}\right)
.
Define
\mathbb{A}
to be the set of possible coupon types.
We note that each coupon allocation is a set of identical coupons, implying that the sets
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathbb{A}
have a one to one correspondence. Consequently, for
{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}
, there exists
a\in\mathbb{A}
such that

{\mathbb{I}}({\mathbf{A}}_{i}={\mathbf{a}})={\mathbb{I}}(A_{i,l}=a)

for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
and
l\in\left\{1,2,\ldots,M_{i}\right\}
. Consequently, we can represent the complete branching process likelihood as

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\kappa}\left(\left\{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}},\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\right)

\displaystyle\triangleq\prod_{v=1}^{\kappa}(2\pi)^{-p/2}\left|\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})\right|^{-1/2}\times

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\left\{({\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}-G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{*}^{R^{v}})^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}_{{\mathbf{a}}}({\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}-G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{*}^{R^{v}})\right\}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{v=1}^{\kappa}(2\pi)^{-p/2}\left|\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})\right|^{-1/2}\times

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\exp\Bigg[-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\Big\{{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}}^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}-2{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{*}^{R^{v}}}^{\top}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}+

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{*}^{R^{v}}}^{\top}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{*}^{R^{v}}\Big\}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})\Bigg].

We will use this likelihood for the proofs that follow.

For
{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}
, we reparameterize
\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}=-\frac{1}{2}\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}=\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\kappa}\left(\left\{\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}},\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{v=1}^{\kappa}(2\pi)^{-p/2}|\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}-2\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})|^{1/2}\times

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\exp\Bigg[\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\Big\{{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}}^{\top}\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}+{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{*}^{R^{v}}}^{\top}\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{\top}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}+

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt{\frac{1}{4}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{*}^{R^{v}}}^{\top}\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{\top}{\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{*}^{R^{v}}\Big\}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})\Bigg].

The reparameterized log-likelihood is

\displaystyle\mathcal{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\kappa}}\left(\left\{\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}},\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{v=1}^{{\kappa}}\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\Bigg[(-p/2)\log(2\pi)+\frac{1}{2}\log|-2\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}|+

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}}^{\top}\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}+{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{*}^{R^{v}}}^{\top}\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{\top}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}+

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\frac{1}{4}{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{*}^{R^{v}}}^{\top}\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{\top}{\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{*}^{R^{v}}\Bigg]\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{v=1}^{{\kappa}}\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\Bigg[(-p/2)\log(2\pi)+\frac{1}{2}\log|-2\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}|+

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\mathrm{tr}\left(\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}}^{\top}\right)+\mathrm{tr}\left(\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{\top}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{*}^{R^{v}}}^{\top}\right)+

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\mathrm{tr}\left(\frac{1}{4}\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{\top}{\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{*}^{R^{v}}{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{*}^{R^{v}}}^{\top}\right)\Bigg]\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}}),

where the second equality follows from rearranging terms and using the properties of the trace operator.
For
{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}
, define
{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}=\sum_{v=1}^{{\kappa}}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})
and
{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{(p+1)\times(p+1)}
such that
{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}=\sum_{v=1}^{{\kappa}}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{*}^{R^{v}}{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{*}^{R^{v}}}^{\top}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})
. We apply the differential operator two times and find

\displaystyle{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}^{2}\mathcal{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\kappa}}\left(\left\{\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}},\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{v=1}^{{\kappa}}\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}-{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}^{2}\Bigg\{-\frac{1}{2}\log|-2\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}|-

\displaystyle\hskip 76.82234pt\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{tr}\left(\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{\top}{\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{*}^{R^{v}}{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{*}^{R^{v}}}^{\top}\right)\Bigg\}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})

\displaystyle=-{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}\Bigg\{-\frac{{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}{2}\mathrm{tr}\left(\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right)-\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{tr}\left(2\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{\top}\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right)+

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{tr}\left(\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{\top}{\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{-1}{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=-\Bigg\{\frac{{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}{2}\mathrm{tr}\left(\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right)-

\displaystyle\hskip 34.14322pt\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr}\left(\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{\top}\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right)-

\displaystyle\hskip 34.14322pt\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr}\left({\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{\top}\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right)+

\displaystyle\hskip 34.14322pt\mathrm{tr}\left(\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{\top}\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right)+

\displaystyle\hskip 34.14322pt\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{tr}\left(\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{\top}{\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{-1}{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right)\Bigg\}.

We observe that for
{\mathbf{a}},{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}
and
{\mathbf{a}}\neq{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}
,

\frac{\partial\mathcal{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\kappa}}\left(\left\{\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}},\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\right)}{\partial\left(\mathrm{vec}(\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}),\mathrm{vec}(\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}})\right)\partial\left(\mathrm{vec}(\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}}),\mathrm{vec}(\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}})\right)}=[0]_{(p+1)^{2}\times p^{2}}.

We express

\displaystyle\ddot{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\kappa}}\left(\left\{\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}},\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{diag}\left[\left\{\begin{pmatrix}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}&2\left({\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{\top}\otimes\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right)\\
2\left({G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right)&4\left({G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{\top}\otimes\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right)+2{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\left(\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right)\end{pmatrix}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\right]

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{diag}\left[\left\{\begin{pmatrix}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}&2{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{\top}\\
2{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}&4{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{\top}+2{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\end{pmatrix}\otimes\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\right].

The second equality follows from properties of kronecker products.

12.2
Consistency of an M-estimator

We establish the conditions for consistency of an M-estimator under a sequence of concave estimating equations. This will be leveraged in Section
12.3
.

Lemma 12.1

(Theorem 2.7 from
Newey and McFadden (1994)
)
.

If there is a function
Q_{0}(\theta)
and sequence of functions
\{\widehat{Q}_{n}(\theta)\}_{n\geq 1}
such that

1.

Q_{0}(\theta)
is uniquely maximized at
\theta_{0}
.

2.

\theta_{0}
is an element of the interior of a convex set
\Theta
.

3.

For all
n\geq 1
,
\widehat{Q}_{n}(\theta)
is concave.

4.

\widehat{Q}_{n}(\theta)\overset{p}{\to}Q_{0}(\theta)
for all
\theta\in\Theta
.

Then,
\widehat{\theta}_{n}=\arg\max_{\theta}\widehat{Q}_{n}(\theta)
exists with probability approaching one, and
\widehat{\theta}_{n}\overset{p}{\to}\theta_{0}
.

12.3
Generalized RDS Inference Proof

We prove consistency of the MLE in this section. First, we establish some helpful lemmas.

Lemma 12.2
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
3
, the log-likelihood for the branching process specified in Equation
6
is concave.

Proof.

Recall that for
{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}
,
\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\triangleq-\frac{1}{2}\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\triangleq\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}
. For
{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}
, define
\kappa_{{\mathbf{a}}}\triangleq\sum_{v=1}^{\kappa}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})
and
{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathbb{R}^{(p+1)\times(p+1)}
such that
{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\triangleq\sum_{v=1}^{\kappa}\left(1,{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{r^{v}}\right)\left(1,{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{r^{v}}\right)^{\top}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})
.
From Section
12.1
, we know that

\displaystyle\ddot{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\kappa}}\left(\left\{\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}},\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{diag}\left[\left\{\begin{pmatrix}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}&2{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{\top}\\
2{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}&4{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{\top}+2{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\end{pmatrix}\otimes\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\right].

We begin by analyzing the quantity,

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}^{{\kappa}}\triangleq\begin{pmatrix}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}&2{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{\top}\\
2{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}&4{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{\top}+2{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\end{pmatrix}.

By Lemma
11.4
, we know that
\mathbb{M}^{{\kappa}}

is positive semi-definite since
{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}
and
4{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{\top}+2{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}-4G{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{-1}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{\top}=2{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}
are positive semi-definite. Consequently, because
\mathbb{M}^{{\kappa}}
and
\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}
are positive semi-definite regardless of
{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}
,

0\succeq\ddot{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\kappa}}\left(\left\{\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}},\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\right).

We conclude that
{\ell}_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left(\left\{\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}},\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\right)/{\kappa}
is concave for all
{\kappa}\in\mathbb{N}
.

\blacksquare

We now show that the Hessian of the log-likelihood is negative definite almost surely under Assumption
17
.

Lemma 12.3
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
3
,
6
,
17
, and Conditions (C2) and (C4),, assume that the true underlying model for RDS is indexed by
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
, and the working model is the branching process specified in Equation
6
.
Define the MLE of the working model covariate distribution as
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\kappa}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})=\arg\max_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\in\mathcal{B}}\ell_{\kappa}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}})
.
For any compact set
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{B}
, there exists
\delta_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}>0
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{\kappa\to\infty}\inf_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\in\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\sigma_{\min}\left(-\ddot{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\kappa}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\right)/\kappa\right)

\displaystyle\geq\delta_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

Proof.

Recall that for
{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}
,
\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\triangleq-\frac{1}{2}\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\triangleq\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}
. For
{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}
, define
\kappa_{{\mathbf{a}}}\triangleq\sum_{v=1}^{\kappa}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})
and
{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathbb{R}^{(p+1)\times(p+1)}
such that
{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\triangleq\sum_{v=1}^{\kappa}\left(1,{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{r^{v}}\right)\left(1,{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{r^{v}}\right)^{\top}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})
.
From Section
12.1
, we know that

\displaystyle\ddot{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\kappa}}\left(\left\{\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}},\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{diag}\left[\left\{\begin{pmatrix}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}&2{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{\top}\\
2{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}&4{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{\top}+2{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\end{pmatrix}\otimes\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\right].

We begin by analyzing the quantity,

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}^{{\kappa}}\triangleq\begin{pmatrix}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}&2{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{\top}\\
2{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}&4{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{\top}+2{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\end{pmatrix}.

By Lemma
11.4
, we know that
\mathbb{M}^{{\kappa}}

is positive semi-definite since
{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}
and
4{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{\top}+2{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}-4G{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{-1}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{\top}=2{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}
are positive semi-definite. Consequently, because
\mathbb{M}^{{\kappa}}
and
\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}
are positive semi-definite regardless of
{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}
,

0\succeq\ddot{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\kappa}}\left(\left\{\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}},\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\right).

We now show that
-\ddot{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\kappa}}
is asymptotically
positive definite
.
By Lemma
11.4
,

\sigma_{\min}\left(\mathbb{M}^{{\kappa}}/{\kappa}\right)\geq\sigma_{\min}\left({\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}/{\kappa}\right)\sigma_{\min}\left(2{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}/{\kappa}\right).

To characterize the convexity of
\mathcal{\ell}_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left(\left\{{G^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{a}}}},\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\right)/{\kappa}
as
{\kappa}\to\infty
, we first analyze

\displaystyle\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}{\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}/{\kappa}

\displaystyle=\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\frac{1}{{\kappa}}\sum_{v=1}^{\kappa}\left(1,{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{R^{v}}\right)\left(1,{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{R^{v}}\right)^{\top}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})

\displaystyle=\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\frac{1}{{\kappa}}\sum_{v=1}^{\kappa}\begin{pmatrix}1&{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{R^{v}}}^{\top}\\
{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{R^{v}}}&{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{R^{v}}{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{R^{v}}}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}}).

Define fields
\mathcal{F}^{\kappa}=\sigma\left[\left\{R^{v},T^{v},{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v},Y^{v},{\mathbf{A}}^{v},C^{v}\right\}_{v=1}^{{\kappa}}\right]
and quantity

\Delta^{v}\triangleq\mathbb{E}\left\{(1,{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{R^{v}})(1,{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{R^{v}}})^{\top}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})\mid\mathcal{F}^{v-1}\right\}.

By Lemma
11.4
,

\displaystyle\Delta^{v}\succ

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{R^{v}}}{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{R^{v}}}^{\top}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})\mid\mathcal{F}^{v-1}\right\}.

Additionally, by Assumption
17
, there exists
N
such that for
{\kappa}\geq N
,
\Delta^{\kappa}\succeq\Delta\succ 0
.

We know that
\forall v\in\mathbb{N}
, every entry of
{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}}^{\top}
is bounded because
\mathcal{X}
is compact.
By Theorem
8.1
,

\displaystyle\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\left\{\sum_{v=1}^{\kappa}\left(1,{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{R^{v}}\right)\left(1,{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{R^{v}}\right)^{\top}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})-\sum_{v=1}^{\kappa}\Delta^{v}\right\}/{\kappa}=0\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

There exists
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\frac{1}{{\kappa}}\sum_{v=1}^{\kappa}\left(1,{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{R^{v}}\right)\left(1,{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{R^{v}}\right)^{\top}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})

\displaystyle=\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\left\{\sum_{v=1}^{\kappa}\left(1,{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{R^{v}}\right)\left(1,{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{R^{v}}\right)^{\top}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})-\sum_{v=1}^{\kappa}\Delta^{v}\right\}/{\kappa}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\sum_{v=1}^{\kappa}\Delta^{v}/{\kappa}

\displaystyle=\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\sum_{v=1}^{\kappa}\Delta^{v}/{\kappa}\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

\displaystyle=\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\sum_{v=N}^{\kappa}\Delta^{v}/\kappa+\sum_{v=1}^{N}\Delta^{v}/N\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

\displaystyle\succeq\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\frac{{\kappa}-N}{{\kappa}}\Delta

\displaystyle\succeq\Delta

Consequently, by the continuous mapping theorem

\displaystyle\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\sigma_{\min}\left\{\frac{1}{{\kappa}}\sum_{v=1}^{\kappa}\left(1,{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{R^{v}}\right)\left(1,{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{R^{v}}\right)^{\top}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})\right\}\geq\sigma_{\min}(\Delta).

Defining
\epsilon_{1}\triangleq\sigma_{\min}(\Delta)>0
, we conclude that
\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\sigma_{\min}\left({\mbox{\boldmath$V$}}^{{\kappa}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}/{\kappa}\right)\geq\epsilon_{1}
a.s. Next, we analyze
2{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}/{\kappa}
. By Assumption
17
and Theorem
8.1
,

\displaystyle\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}/{\kappa}

\displaystyle=\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\left\{\sum_{v=1}^{\kappa}\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}})-\mathbb{P}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}}\mid\mathcal{F}^{{\kappa}-1})\right\}/{\kappa}+\sum_{v=1}^{\kappa}\mathbb{P}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}}\mid\mathcal{F}^{{\kappa}-1})/{\kappa}

\displaystyle\geq\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\sum_{v=1}^{\kappa}\mathbb{P}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}}\mid\mathcal{F}^{{\kappa}-1})/{\kappa}

\displaystyle\geq\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\sum_{v=N}^{\kappa}\mathbb{P}({\mathbf{A}}^{R^{v}}={\mathbf{a}}\mid\mathcal{F}^{{\kappa}-1})/{\kappa}

\displaystyle\geq\delta\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

Consequently,
\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}/{\kappa}\geq\delta
a.s. as
{\kappa}\to\infty
.

Therefore,

\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\sigma_{\min}\left(2{\kappa}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}/{\kappa}\right)\geq 2\sigma_{\min}(\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}})\delta\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

Because the minimum eigenvalue of a matrix is a continuous function, by the extreme value theorem, we know that for some
\alpha>0
,

\min_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\min_{\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\in\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\sigma_{\min}(\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}})\geq\alpha.

Defining
\epsilon_{2}\triangleq 2\alpha\delta\epsilon_{1}
, we find that

\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\sigma_{\min}\left(\mathbb{M}^{\kappa}/{\kappa}\right)\geq\epsilon_{2}\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

Defining
\epsilon_{3}\triangleq\epsilon_{2}\alpha
, for any
{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}
, we find that

\displaystyle\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\sigma_{\min}\left\{-\ddot{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\kappa}}\left(\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}},\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right)\right\}/{\kappa}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\min}\left(\mathbb{M}^{\kappa}\otimes\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\min}\left(\mathbb{M}^{\kappa}\right)\sigma_{\min}\left(\Sigma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\epsilon_{3}\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

Line 2 follows because for square matrices
A
and
B
,
\sigma_{\min}(A\otimes B)=\sigma_{\min}(A)\sigma_{\min}(B)
.
In conclusion,

\displaystyle\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\sigma_{\min}\left\{\ddot{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\kappa}}\left(\left\{\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}},\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\right)/{\kappa}\right\}

\displaystyle=\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\prod_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\sigma_{\min}\left\{\ddot{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\kappa}}\left(\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}},\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right)\right\}/{\kappa}

\displaystyle\leq-\epsilon_{3}^{|\mathcal{A}|}\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

\displaystyle<0\ \ \mathrm{a.s.},

where the first line follows from Lemma
11.4
(where the off-diagonal blocks are zero).
We complete the proof by defining
\delta_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\triangleq\epsilon_{3}
.

\blacksquare

We verify the last part of Assumption
16
.

Lemma 12.4
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
3
,
6
,
17
, and Conditions (C2) and (C4), assume that the true underlying model for RDS is indexed by
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
, and the working model is the branching process specified in Equation
6
. For any
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\in\mathcal{B}
,

\dot{\ell}_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right)/{\kappa}=O_{p}(1)

as
\kappa\to\infty
.

Proof.

Write

\dot{\ell}_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right)=\sum_{v=1}^{\kappa}q^{v}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\right)=\sum_{v=1}^{\kappa}q\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}},\left\{Y^{v},{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v},T^{v},R^{v},{\mathbf{A}}^{v}\right\}\right).

We see from Section
12.1
that the function
q
is continuous over the compact space
\mathcal{Y}\times\mathcal{X}\times[t_{\min},t_{\max}]\times\left\{1,2,\ldots v\right\}\times\mathcal{A}
(which defines the data space). Consequently, for any given value of
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}
,
q
has a maximum over possible data values. Call this maximum
q^{*}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}})
. Consequently,

\dot{\ell}_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right)/{\kappa}=\sum_{v=1}^{\kappa}q\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}},\left\{Y^{v},{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v},T^{v},R^{v},{\mathbf{A}}^{v}\right\}\right)\leq\sum_{v=1}^{\kappa}q^{*}/\kappa=q^{*}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}).

The lemma follows.

\blacksquare

Lemmas
12.2
-
12.4
verify Assumption
16
under the working model specified by Equation
6
, Assumptions
1
-
3
,
6
,
17
, and Conditions (C2) and (C4).
We now prove Theorem
3.1
under Assumption
16
(which suffices to prove Theorem
3.1
under both assumption sets).

Proof of Theorem
3.1
.

Proof of consistency.

Because
\mathcal{B}
is open, we know that
\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})
is an element of the interior of
\mathcal{B}
. Consequently, there exists
\gamma^{*}>0
such that

N_{\gamma^{*}}\triangleq\left\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}:\left\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\|_{2}\leq\gamma^{*}\right\}

is a compact subset of
\mathcal{B}
.

Under Assumption
16
, we know that
{\ell}_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}})/{\kappa}
is concave for all
{\kappa}\in\mathbb{N}
, and that for
\gamma^{*}>0
there exists a
\delta>0
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{\kappa\to\infty}\inf_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in N_{\gamma^{*}}}\sigma_{\min}\left(-\ddot{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\kappa}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\right)/\kappa\right)

\displaystyle\geq\delta\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

(34)

We now show that for
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\in N_{\gamma^{*}}
,
\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\right)/{\kappa}
is strictly concave asymptotically. For any
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}\in N_{\gamma^{*}}
and
t\in[0,1]
, we set
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger}=t{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}+(1-t){\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}
. For
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime\prime}\in\left\{\alpha{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}+(1-\alpha){\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger}:\alpha\in[0,1]\right\}
,
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\left\{\alpha{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger}+(1-\alpha){\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}:\alpha\in[0,1]\right\}
, and an
\epsilon\in(0,\delta)
, there exists
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\forall{\kappa}\geq N
(by Taylor expansion),

\displaystyle\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right)/{\kappa}

\displaystyle=\left\{\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger}\right)+\dot{\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger}\right)^{\top}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger})+\frac{1}{2}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger})^{\top}\ddot{\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime\prime}\right)({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger})\right\}/{\kappa}

\displaystyle\leq\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger}\right)/{\kappa}+\dot{\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger}\right)^{\top}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger})/{\kappa}-\frac{1}{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger}\|_{2}^{2}\epsilon\ \ \mathrm{a.s.},

and

\displaystyle\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}\right)/{\kappa}

\displaystyle=\left\{\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger}\right)+\dot{\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger}\right)^{\top}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger})+\frac{1}{2}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger})^{\top}\ddot{\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger})\right\}/{\kappa}

\displaystyle\leq\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger}\right)/{\kappa}+\dot{\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger}\right)^{\top}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger})/{\kappa}-\frac{1}{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger}\|_{2}^{2}\epsilon\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

Both of these inequalities follow because (1) for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and semi-positive definite matrix
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, we know that
x^{\top}Ax\geq\sigma_{\min}(A)x^{\top}x
and (2) we have a lower bound on
-\ddot{\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right)/\kappa
for any
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\in N_{\gamma^{*}}
by Equation
34
.

From these two expressions, we find that
\forall{\kappa}\geq N
,

\displaystyle t\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right)/{\kappa}+(1-t)\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}\right)/{\kappa}

\displaystyle\leq\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger}\right)/{\kappa}-

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\frac{1}{2}\left\{t\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger}\|_{2}^{2}+(1-t)\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\epsilon\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

\displaystyle=\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger}\right)/{\kappa}-\frac{1}{2}\Big\{t(1-t)^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}+

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt(1-t)t^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}\Big\}\epsilon\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

\displaystyle=\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger}\right)/{\kappa}-\frac{1}{2}t(1-t)\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}\epsilon\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

Lines 1, 2, and 3 follow from distributing, combining like terms, and refactoring (as well as the inequalities stated above). By point-wise convergence, this implies that

\displaystyle t\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right)+(1-t)\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}\right)\leq\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\dagger}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left\{t(1-t)\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\epsilon\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

Consequently, we know that
\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}
is strictly concave a.s. over the compact set
N_{\gamma^{*}}
; i.e., for any
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}\in N_{\gamma^{*}}
and
t\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle t\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right)+(1-t)\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}\right)<\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left(t{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}+(1-t){\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}\right).

Lastly, we show that strict concavity over
N_{\gamma^{*}}
implies that
\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\in\arg\max_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\in\mathcal{B}}\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}})
is unique. For a proof by contradiction, assume that
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\in\mathcal{B}
such that
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\neq\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})
and
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\in\arg\max_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\in\mathcal{B}}\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}})
as well. By concavity, for any
t\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}

\displaystyle=t\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}+(1-t)\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}})

\displaystyle\leq\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{t\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})+(1-t){\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\}.

Because
\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})
and
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}
are maxima, this implies that for any
t\in(0,1)
,

\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}=\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\right)=\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{t\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})+(1-t){\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\}.

For any
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\notin N_{\gamma^{*}}
, we find that for
t\geq 1-\gamma^{*}/\|\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\|_{2}
,

\displaystyle\|\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\left(t\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})+(1-t){\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right)\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|(1-t)\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-(1-t){\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=(1-t)\|\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{*}.

We now pick any
t^{*}=\max\left[1-\gamma^{*}/\left\{2\|\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\|_{2}\right\},0\right]
and label
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}^{\prime}=t^{*}\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})+(1-t^{*}){\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}
. We know that
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}^{\prime}\in N_{\gamma^{*}}
, and
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}^{\prime}\in\arg\max_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\in\mathcal{B}}\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}})
. Additionally,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}&=\frac{1}{2}\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}+\frac{1}{2}\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime})\\
&<\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\frac{1}{2}\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})+\frac{1}{2}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}\right\},\end{split}

(35)

where the last inequality is strict because
\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}
is strictly concave over
N_{\gamma^{*}}
.
Equation
35
is a
contradiction
because
(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})+{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime})/2
has a higher log-likelihood than
\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})
. Consequently,
\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})
must be unique.

We now know that
\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}
has a unique maximizer and
\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}})/{\kappa}
is concave for all
{\kappa}\in\mathbb{N}
.
By Lemma
12.1
,
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{{\kappa}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\overset{P}{\to}\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})
.

Ridge regression penalty.

We also prove consistency under a ridge regression penalty. The log-likelihood with a ridge regression component parameterized by
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
is

\displaystyle\mathcal{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\alpha}_{{\kappa}}\left(\left\{\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}},\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\kappa}}\left(\left\{\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}},\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right\}_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\right)-\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\alpha}{2}\mathrm{tr}\left(\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{\top}{\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right).

Define
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{{\kappa}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}},\alpha)=\arg\max_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\in\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\alpha}_{{\kappa}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right)
. We want to prove that
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{{\kappa}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}},\alpha)\overset{p}{\to}\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})
as
{\kappa}\to\infty
.
Since
-\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{\top}{\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}
is negative semi-definite, we know that
-\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\alpha}{2}\mathrm{tr}\left(\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{\top}{\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right)
is concave. We conclude that
\ell^{\alpha}_{{\kappa}}
is concave for all
{\kappa}\in\mathbb{N}
since it is the sum of two concave functions. Additionally, since

\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\alpha}{2}\mathrm{tr}\left(\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}^{\top}{\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right)/{\kappa}=0,

we know that for any
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\in\mathcal{B}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\mathcal{\ell}^{\alpha}_{{\kappa}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right)/{\kappa}=\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\left[\mathcal{\ell}_{{\kappa}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right)/{\kappa}-\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\alpha}{2}\mathrm{tr}\left({\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{\top}{\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{{\mathbf{a}}}\right)/{\kappa}\right]=\overline{\ell}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}).

Using Lemma
12.1
, we conclude that
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{{\kappa}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}},\alpha)\overset{p}{\to}\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})
.

Concentration.

We now prove the concentration statement of Theorem
3.1
: for any
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\notin\Theta^{*}
,

\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left\{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in\Gamma_{1-\alpha,\kappa}\right\}\to 0.

Recall that
\Theta^{*}=\left\{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in\Theta:\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})=\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*})\right\}
. For a given
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in\Theta
, we define the sampling distribution of the log-likelihood ratio statistic as
P_{\kappa}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\right)
such that

-2\left[\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}-\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}\right]\sim P_{\kappa}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\right).

Furthermore, define the
1-\alpha
quantile of
P_{\kappa}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\right)
as
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{1-\alpha,\kappa}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\right)
and the confidence set in question as

\Gamma_{1-\alpha,\kappa}=\left\{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}:-2\left[\ell_{{\kappa}}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}-\ell_{{\kappa}}\left\{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\}\right]\leq{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{1-\alpha,\kappa}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\right)\right\}.

We know that
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}
is consistent for
\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*})
. Therefore, we know that for any
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that
\left\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}:\left\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*}\right)\right\|_{2}\leq\epsilon_{0}\right\}\subset\mathcal{B}
, there exists
N_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for all
{\kappa}\geq N_{1}
,
\left\|\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*}\right)\right\|\leq\epsilon_{0}
almost surely.

Define the compact set
\mathcal{B}_{c}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}=\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)\cup\left\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}:\left\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\left({\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*}\right)\right\|_{2}\leq\epsilon_{0}\right\}
. Additionally, for any
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}^{p},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}^{g}\in\mathcal{B}_{c}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}
, define
\mathcal{B}_{*}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}=\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\in\left\{\alpha{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}^{p}+(1-\alpha){\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}^{g}:\alpha\in[0,1]\right\}
. Note that
\mathcal{B}_{*}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}
is compact because it is the image of a continuous function on a compact set in Euclidean space.

Under Assumption
16
, we know that there exists an
N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for an
\epsilon_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}>0
and any
{\kappa}\geq N_{2}

\displaystyle\inf_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\in\mathcal{B}_{*}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}}\sigma_{\min}\left\{-\ddot{\ell}_{{\kappa}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right)/{\kappa}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\epsilon_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

Consequently, for all
{\kappa}\geq\max\{N_{1},N_{2}\}
, any
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in\Theta
, and a

{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}\in\left\{\alpha\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}+(1-\alpha)\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}):\alpha\in[0,1]\right\}
,

\displaystyle-2\left[\ell_{{\kappa}}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}-\ell_{{\kappa}}\left\{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\}\right]/{\kappa}

\displaystyle=2\left[\ell_{{\kappa}}\left\{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\}-\ell_{{\kappa}}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}\right]/{\kappa}

\displaystyle=2\Big[\ell_{{\kappa}}\left\{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\}-\ell_{{\kappa}}\left\{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\}-\dot{\ell}_{{\kappa}}\left\{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\}^{\top}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\}-

\displaystyle\hskip 19.91684pt\frac{1}{2}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\}^{\top}\ddot{\ell}_{{\kappa}}\left\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}\right\}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\}\Big]/{\kappa}

\displaystyle=\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\}^{\top}\left\{-\ddot{\ell}_{{\kappa}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}\right)/{\kappa}\right\}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\epsilon_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\|\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

Line 3 follows from an exact Taylor expansion. Line 4 follows from the fact that

\dot{\ell}_{{\kappa}}\left\{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\}=0
. Line 5 follows from the fact that
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{*}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}
.

For
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime\prime}\in\left\{\alpha\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})+(1-\alpha)\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}):\alpha\in[0,1]\right\}
, we now upper bound

\displaystyle 0\leq-2\left[\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}-\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}\right]/{\kappa}

\displaystyle=2\left[\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}-\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}\right]/{\kappa}

\displaystyle=2\Big[\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}-\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}+\dot{\ell}_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}^{\top}\left\{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}+

\displaystyle\hskip 19.91684pt\frac{1}{2}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}^{\top}\ddot{\ell}_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime\prime}\right\}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}\Big]/{\kappa}

\displaystyle=2\left[\dot{\ell}_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}/{\kappa}\right]^{\top}\left\{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}-

\displaystyle\hskip 19.91684pt\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}^{\top}\left[-\ddot{\ell}_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\prime\prime}\right\}/{\kappa}\right]\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left[\dot{\ell}_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}/{\kappa}\right]^{\top}\left\{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

Lines 1, 2, and 3 follow from simple algebra. Line 4 follows from the fact that we know from the same logic as Lemma
12.3
that
\ell_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}
is concave for any
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in\Theta
.

By Assumption
16
, we know that
\dot{\ell}_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}/{\kappa}=O_{p}(1)
as
\kappa\to\infty
.
Because

\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\left\|\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\|_{2}^{2}=0
a.s., we know that

\displaystyle\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\left(2\left[\dot{\ell}_{{\kappa}}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}/{\kappa}\right]^{\top}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}\right)=0.\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

Therefore, we know that
\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{1-\alpha,\kappa}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\right)/{\kappa}=0
a.s. We find that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1-\alpha,\kappa}

\displaystyle\triangleq\left\{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}:-2\left[\ell_{{\kappa}}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}-\ell_{{\kappa}}\left\{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\}\right]\leq{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{1-\alpha,\kappa}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}:-2\left[\ell_{{\kappa}}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}-\ell_{{\kappa}}\left\{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\}\right]/{\kappa}\leq{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{1-\alpha,\kappa}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\right)/{\kappa}\right\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\left\{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}:\epsilon_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\|\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{1-\alpha,\kappa}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\right)/{\kappa}\right\}\ \mathrm{a.s.}

Line 2 follows by dividing both sides of the inequality by
{\kappa}
. Line 3 follows because for any
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in\Theta
,

\displaystyle-2\left[\ell_{{\kappa}}\left\{\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}-\ell_{{\kappa}}\left\{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\}\right]/{\kappa}\geq\epsilon_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\|\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\ \ \mathrm{a.s.}

as
{\kappa}\to\infty
. Because
\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}=\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*})
, we know that if
\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*})\neq\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})
,

\epsilon_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\|\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\to\epsilon_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\|\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*})\right\|_{2}^{2}>0.

Additionally,
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{1-\alpha,\kappa}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\right)/{\kappa}\to 0
.
Consequently, for any
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\notin\Theta^{*}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left({\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in\Gamma_{1-\alpha,\kappa}\right)\leq\lim_{{\kappa}\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\epsilon_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\|\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{{\kappa}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{1-\alpha,\kappa}^{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}}\right)/\kappa\right)\to 0.

\blacksquare

13
Branching Model Paradigm for RL-RDS

In this section, we conduct a series of simulation experiments to evaluate the operating characteristics
of RL-RDS when the branching process is the true generative model. To allow comparisons with the two-stage procedure proposed by

McFall et al. (2021)
(see also
VanOrsdale (2023)
),
we consider the setting in which the goal is to recruit the largest subset of
people in a hidden population with a given binary trait, e.g., undiagnosed HIV.
The outcome is thus an indicator of this trait.
We estimate the optimal policy,
\boldsymbol{\pi}^{\mathrm{opt}}
, using RL-RDS. In this paradigm, we assume that the branching process described in Section
2.2
is the true generative model. We restate this model here for convenience.

Recall that
T_{i,l}
,
{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i,l}
,
Y_{i,l}
, and
A_{i,l}
for
l=1,\ldots,M_{i}
are the arrival times, covariates, rewards, and coupon types associated with the potential recruits of recruiter
i
respectively.
Treating
A_{i,l}
as a factor, let
{\mbox{\boldmath$Z$}}_{i,l}\in\mathcal{Z}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
be a row in the model matrix of a model that includes a main effect,
{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i,l}
and its interaction with
A_{i,l}
.
For example, if
\mathcal{A}=\{-1,1\}
, then
{\mbox{\boldmath$Z$}}_{i,l}=\left(1,{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i,l},{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i,l}{\mathbb{I}}\left(A_{i,l}=-1\right)\right)
. Define
\mathbb{A}
as the set of possible coupon types.

We consider a working model of the form (same as Equation
6
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(M_{i}=m_{i}|{\mbox{\boldmath$H$}}_{i},{\mathbf{A}}_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{m_{i}}/m_{i}!}{\sum_{\ell=0}^{|\mathbf{A}_{i}|}(\lambda^{\ell}/\ell!)},m_{i}=0,\ldots,|\mathbf{A}_{i}|,

\displaystyle T_{i,l}-T_{i}|{\mbox{\boldmath$H$}}_{i},{\mathbf{A}}_{i},M_{i}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\mathrm{Truncated\ Exponential}(\zeta,t_{\min},t_{\max}),\,l=1,\ldots,M_{i},

\displaystyle{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i,l}|{\mbox{\boldmath$H$}}_{i},U_{i,l},{\mathbf{A}}_{i},M_{i}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\mathrm{Normal}\left(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{a}+G_{a}{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i},\Sigma_{a}\right),\,l=1,\ldots,M_{i},a=a_{i,l}

\displaystyle Y_{i,l}|{\mbox{\boldmath$H$}}_{i},{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}_{i},U_{i,l},{\mathbf{A}}_{i},M_{i}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\mathrm{Bernoulli}\left\{\frac{1}{1+\exp\left(-{{\mbox{\boldmath$Z$}}_{i,l}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{y}\right)}\right\},\,j=1,\ldots,M_{i},

where
\lambda,\zeta\in\mathbb{R}
,
\{G_{a}\}_{a\in\mathbb{A}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
,

\{\boldsymbol{\phi}_{a}\}_{a\in\mathbb{A}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times 1}
,
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
,
and
\{\Sigma_{a}\}_{a\in\mathbb{A}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
.
We draw the covariates of the initial sample,
\mathcal{E}_{0}
, from a multivariate normal distribution,
\mathrm{Normal}\left(\mu,\Sigma\right)
.

13.1
Policies

We evaluate the performance of RL-RDS against a suite of alternative strategies. At each step, the researcher can choose from a finite selection of coupon types.
The fixed allocation policies (i.e., those that give the same coupon allocation type to all participants) represent the current standard in RDS. The train and implement policy mimics the procedure used by
McFall et al. (2021)
, which determines an incentive strategy using a pilot study.
We describe each policy below.

1.

Fixed
offers a fixed coupon allocation
{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{A}
to every study participant. If
{\mathbf{a}}\notin\psi^{v}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{v})
, then pick a random coupon allocation from
\psi^{v}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{v})
to give to the
v^{\mathrm{th}}
study participant.

2.

Random
offers a random element of
\psi^{v}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{v})
to the
v^{\mathrm{th}}
study participant.

3.

Train and Implement
uses half of the budget for a “pilot study,” in which the the Random policy is used to assign coupon allocations. It then conducts policy search using the pilot study data to estimate the branching process working model. This estimated policy (without updating) is used to determine coupon allocations for the remainder of the budget.

4.

RL-RDS
uses the Random policy to assign coupon allocations to participants in a short “warm-up” period (
50
participants in the simulations below). Then, it performs policy search with Thompson sampling as outlined in Section
2
for the remainder of the budget.

To conduct RL-RDS, we establish a reasonable space of policies,
\Pi
. Define
\alpha_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,
\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, and
\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}=(\alpha_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{1})
. For
n\in\mathbb{N}
and state
{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n}\in\mathcal{H}^{n}
, we consider policies of the form
\boldsymbol{\pi}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n})=\left\{\pi^{n}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n}),\pi^{n+1}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n+1}),\cdots\right\}
such that for
v\geq n
,

\pi^{v}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{v})=\pi^{v}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{v},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})=\pi^{v}({\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{v},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})=g^{v}\left[\frac{1}{1+\exp\left\{-(\alpha_{0}+{{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{v}}^{\top}\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{1})\right\}}\right],

where
g^{v}:(0,1)\to\phi^{v}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{v})
maps a continuous score (dependent on the participant’s covariates) to a coupon allocation,
{\mathbf{a}}^{v}\in\psi^{v}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{v})
.
We first draw
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{n}
from the MLE sampling distribution using a generalized bootstrap for estimating equations
(
Chatterjee and Bose, 2005
)
. We then generate synthetic data sets,

\mathcal{K}^{B}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n},\boldsymbol{\pi};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{n})=\left\{\left({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}_{b}^{n},A_{b}^{n},Y_{b}^{n},{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}_{b}^{n+1},A_{b}^{n+1},Y_{b}^{n+1},\ldots,{\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}_{b}^{Q},Y_{b}^{Q}\right)\right\}_{b=1}^{B},

and calculate

\widehat{V}^{n}_{B}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n},\boldsymbol{\pi};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{n})=\frac{\sum_{b=1}^{B}\left\{\sum_{v\geq n}\Delta^{v}_{b}Y^{v}_{b}\right\}}{B}

for each
\boldsymbol{\pi}\in\Pi
(or each
\boldsymbol{\pi}
in a grid approximation of
\Pi
). To determine the coupon allocation given to the latest study participant, we set
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}_{B}^{n}=\arg\max_{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}\widehat{V}^{n}_{B}({\mbox{\boldmath$h$}}^{n},{\boldsymbol{\alpha}};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}^{n})
,
and assign
{\mathbf{a}}^{n}=\pi^{n}({\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{n},\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}_{B}^{n})
.

13.2
Results

In the following simulations, we set the hidden population size to
N=5,000
. The recruitment process begins with an initial sample of
25
individuals randomly drawn from the population,
\left|\mathcal{E}_{0}\right|=25
with
\mu^{*}=(1,1,1)^{\top}
and
\Sigma^{*}=\mathrm{diag}(1/8)
. We define

\displaystyle G_{a_{1}}=\begin{pmatrix}\phi_{1}&0&0\\
0&\phi_{1}&0\\
0&0&\phi_{1}\end{pmatrix},G_{a_{2}}=\begin{pmatrix}\phi_{1}*0.975&0&0\\
0&\phi_{1}*0.975&0\\
0&0&\phi_{1}*0.975\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle G_{a_{3}}=\begin{pmatrix}\phi_{1}*0.95&0&0\\
0&\phi_{1}*0.95&0\\
0&0&\phi_{1}*0.95\end{pmatrix},\ \boldsymbol{\phi}_{a_{1}}=\boldsymbol{\phi}_{a_{2}}=\boldsymbol{\phi}_{a_{3}}=\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0},

where we set
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}=\left(0.05,0.05,0.05\right)
and
\phi_{1}=0.95
in our weak correlation setting, and we set
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}=\left(0.025,0.025,0.025\right)
and
\phi_{1}=0.975
in our strong correlation setting.
The researchers have access to three types of coupon allocations
\{{\mathbf{a}}_{1},{\mathbf{a}}_{2},{\mathbf{a}}_{3}\}
, and each allocation has
5
coupons. We found that limiting the number of coupons given to each study participant in the pilot study and the warm-up period of the T&I and the RLRDS policies respectively allows us to observe the effects of the learned policies earlier in the sampling process. Consequently, we give two coupons to individuals in the pilot and warm-up period, and increase the allotment to
5
coupons afterwards. Additionally,
C^{v}\equiv 1
for all
v\in\mathbb{N}
. Define the reward model components as
{\mbox{\boldmath$Z$}}_{j}^{v}\triangleq\left\{1,{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}_{j},\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{v}_{j}={\mathbf{a}}_{2}){\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}_{j},\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{A}}^{v}_{j}={\mathbf{a}}_{3}){\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}_{j}\right\}
and
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{y}^{*}\triangleq(-1,3{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}},-3{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}},-6{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}})
, where
{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}\triangleq(1,-1,-1)
.
The basic policy objective is clear: we want to ensure that coupon type 1,
{\mathbf{a}}_{1}
, is used to recruit individuals with covariates that satisfy
{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}}^{\top}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}>0
, and coupon type 3,
{\mathbf{a}}_{3}
, is used to recruit individuals with covariates that satisfy
{{\mbox{\boldmath$X$}}^{v}}^{\top}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}<0
.
We define the policy space by specifying the function
g^{v}
,

g^{v}(z)=\begin{cases}{\mathbf{a}}_{1},&\mathrm{if}\ z>0.66,\\
{\mathbf{a}}_{2},&\mathrm{if}\ 0.66>z\geq 0.33,\\
{\mathbf{a}}_{3},&\mathrm{if}\ 0.33>z.\end{cases}

This policy space implies that correctly assigning coupons
1
or
3
will depend on the sign of the
{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
components. The frequency of coupon 2 allocation will be determined by the magnitude of
{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
. This structure makes finding an optimal policy computationally feasible while maintaining sufficient difficulty to showcase the strength of RL-RDS. Lastly, we set
\lambda=3
,
\zeta=0.5
,
t_{\min}=0
, and
t_{\max}=3
.

Figure
3
illustrates the estimated value of policies in both the sparse and dense network settings. It indicates that RL-RDS outperforms all competitor policies by a significant margin in each regime.
